# BESS constants register — sourced numbers for GB behind-the-meter modelling

Every reusable number the literature survey captured, with its units, the conditions it
applies under, its vintage and its source. Built repo-blind (see
[`bess_modelling_requirements.md`](bess_modelling_requirements.md) §0).

**Rules for using this register.**

- A number is only usable with its **basis**. Most errors here come from taking a value
  whose conditions do not match the case — a de-rating factor from the 
wrong auction year, a
  skip rate from the wrong methodology stage, a capture rate from a different duration.
- Where sources **conflict**, both rows are kept. Nothing is averaged.
- **Vintages matter enormously in GB.** The Targeted Charging Review, the 
frequency-response
  product set and the Open Balancing Platform all changed the answer within the last few
  years, and BM skip rates have roughly halved between 2023 and 2026. A number without a
  vintage is not usable.

---

## Behind-the-meter C&I storage: value stacking, conflicts and empirical outcomes (Great Britain, 0.1–5 MW, 1–4 h, sometimes solar co-located)

| Dynamic Services minimum response capacity | 1 MW minimum, 100 MW maximum, per response unit; sell orders in whole MW only | MW | GB, DC/DM/DR, guidance version June 2026. Aggregation permitted only where assets sit behind the same GSP Group, subject to technical assurance. Participation open to both BM and non-BM registered assets. | NESO, 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance', v14, June 2026, slide 6, https://www.neso.energy/document/276606/download | This is the binding eligibility constraint for BTM assets under 1 MW — the great majority of the 0.1–5 MW C&I segment cannot access DC/DM/DR standalone. |

DM responds between ±0.1 Hz and ±0.2 Hz; DR reaches full delivery at ±0.2 Hz. | NESO, 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance', v14, June 2026, Table 3, https://www.neso.energy/document/276606/download | Used to estimate energy actually moved (and therefore degradation) while providing an availability-only service. |

The word 'commercial' was added downstream in this register and must not be relied on: the range is domestic retail pricing for a 20 kWh system in 2021 GBP and is NOT transferable to a 100-750 kW C&I install. NPV was positive only up to a capital cost of approximately £490/kWh | $/kWh; £/kWh; £ NPV; years | GB, 10 kW / 20 kWh system, 2020–2022 price vintage. | Seward, W., Qadrdan, M., Jenkins, N., PSCC 2022, §IV.A.2, https://pscc-central.epfl.ch/repo/papers/2022/19890.pdf | The authors conclude that 'despite revenue stacking improving investment viability of BSSs, the cost of small-scale BSSs will limit their mass adoption'. Prices have fallen since 2022 — re-source current £/kWh for the actual size band before use. **The modelled system is 10 kW / 20 kWh at a school in Cardiff**, so neither the £445-1,315/kWh range nor the £490/kWh break-even is a C&I figure; and the £490/kWh break-even is for the arbitrage+DC scenario, which needs Dynamic Containment, whose 1 MW minimum this segment cannot meet. Misquotation caught and corrected 2026-08-17 (Olly). |
| Calendar and cyclic degradation model (GB BTM case study) | Calendar: 0.00274%/day (20% capacity loss over a 20-year shelf life to 80% EOL). Cyclic: N_t = 12198·DoD³ + 34954·DoD² − 97517·DoD + 52895 cycles; D_cyc = 0.5·\|1/N_t − 1/N_{t−1}\|. Daily degradation D_day = max(D_calendar, D_cyclic). End of life at 80% of original energy capacity | %/day; cycles; dimensionless | Lithium-ion stationary BSS. DoD_t = (E_max − E_t)/E_max. Cycle-depth curve from a manufacturer characteristic. | Seward, W., Qadrdan, M., Jenkins, N., PSCC 2022, eqns (13)–(18), https://pscc-central.epfl.ch/repo/papers/2022/19890.pdf | The authors note stationary Li-ion BSS typically reach end of life at 70–80% of starting energy capacity, so 80% is the conservative end of the published range. |
| Cycle depth sensitivity of lithium-ion degradation | An NMC battery incurs approximately ten times more degradation when operated at near 100% cycle depth of discharge compared with 10% DoD, for the SAME amount of charged energy or energy throughput. A common literature simplification is a constant degradation price of 2 $/MWh based on energy throughput | ratio; $/MWh | Electrochemical/semi-empirical rainflow cycle-counting framework; NMC chemistry cited as the example. | Shi, Y., Xu, B., Tan, Y., Kirschen, D., Zhang, B., 'Optimal Battery Control Under Cycle Aging Mechanisms', arXiv:1709.05715v2, 2017, §II, https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.05715 | This is the key argument against throughput-only degradation costing. The rainflow cost function f(c,d) = ΔL(c,d)·E·B (E in kWh, B in $/kWh) is proved convex, so it can be embedded directly in an optimisation. |
| Rainflow cycle-based life-loss formulation | ΔL(v,w) = Σ_i Φ(v_i)/2 + Σ_i Φ(w_i)/2, where v and w are vectors of charging and discharging half-cycle depths from the rainflow algorithm, and Φ(u) = α·u^β (convex) is the cycle-depth stress function. Cost f(c,d) = ΔL(c,d) · E · B | fractional life loss; £ or $ via cell cost B in $/kWh and capacity E in kWh | General lithium-ion; cycle depths recovered from the SoC profile via (v,w) = Rainflow(τη_c/E · c − τ/(η_d E) · d). | Shi, Y., Xu, B., Tan, Y., Kirschen, D., Zhang, B., arXiv:1709.05715v2, 2017, eqns (3)–(6), https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.05715 | The paper focuses on cycle depth and explicitly excludes temperature, C-rate and cell imbalance on the grounds that in grid applications these are controlled within a narrow band. |
| Value of optimised versus rule-based BTM dispatch (real microgrid) | Monthly demand charges reduced by approximately 45%. Over one summer: total final cost $17,952.05 (rule-based benchmark) versus $6,654.59 (stochastic MILP), a difference of $11,294.49. The coincident-peak component alone moved from a $931.76 cost to a $4,116.17 reward, a swing of $5,029.93 | USD; % reduction | Real anonymised microgrid in PSE&G / PJM territory, summer 2023, 1000 MWh / 500 MW nominal BESS parameters, hourly resolution, SOC bounds 20%–96%, round-trip efficiency 0.8. | Carmona, R., Yang, X., Bhela, S., Zeng, C., 'Maximizing on-bill savings through battery management optimization', arXiv:2409.03942, September 2024, Table 3, https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03942 | US tariff structure (1CP transmission, 5CP capacity, monthly NCP demand charge), not GB. Use for the MAGNITUDE of the control-quality effect, not for GB revenue levels. The benchmark is a rational rule-based strategy, not a naive one, so the gap is a lower bound on the value of good control. |
| Temporal resolution effect on peak shaving | In the power-constrained region, the difference between the 15-minute peak and the 1-hour average peak persists in the optimised net load until battery power capacity is sufficiently large. In the energy-constrained region the sequence effect causes energy-constrained batteries to UNDERESTIMATE peak shaving and demand charge reduction | qualitative with regime boundaries defined by critical power (CP) and critical energy (CE) | Real commercial building load, 15-minute versus 1-hour resolution, linear program with perfect forecasts; battery rating space divided into optimal, power-constrained and energy-constrained regions. | Liu, S., 'Battery Operating Regimes: Effect of Temporal Resolution on Peak Shaving by Battery Energy Storage System', MSc thesis, University of California San Diego, 2021, abstract, https://escholarship.org/uc/item/2gp2v64w | US 15-minute demand-charge convention; GB settlement is half-hourly. The direction of the error is regime-dependent, so a single 'coarse data overstates value' heuristic is wrong. |
| P415 (VLP wholesale market access) key dates | Modification raised 30 September 2020; BSC Panel recommendation 8 June 2023; Ofgem decision published 6 October 2023; implementation date 7 November 2024 | dates | GB Balancing and Settlement Code. Introduces 'Deviation Volumes'; suppliers commercially impacted by VLP action are compensated at a price representing average supplier sourcing costs calculated using Ofgem's Price Cap Methodology; under the approved Proposed Solution those costs are mutualised amongst suppliers. | Ofgem, 'Decision on BSC modification P415: Facilitating access to wholesale markets for flexibility dispatched by Virtual Lead Parties', 6 October 2023, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/publications/decision-bsc-modification-p415; Elexon, https://www.elexon.co.uk/bsc/mod-proposal/p415/ | Before P415, 'the BSC currently assigns all flexibility delivered by a consumer to their supplier... consumers can only access the wholesale market through their supplier'. Any model that assumed independent BTM wholesale trading before 7 November 2024 was wrong. |
| EREC G100 applicability date and voltage scope | Applies to customers' installations at any voltage where new load or generation equipment is commissioned on or after 1 May 2023. Focus is installations connected at voltages up to and including 20 kV | date; kV | GB. Issue 2 became mandatory on 1 May 2023, when Issue 1 Amendment 2 was withdrawn. | Energy Networks Association, EREC G100 Issue 2 Amendment 2 (2023), Foreword and §1, https://www.energynetworks.org/publications/ena-erec-g100-issue-2-amendment-2-technical-requirements-customer-export-and-import-limitation-schemes | Above 20 kV the same principles apply but the DNO may vary exact requirements to suit local network characteristics. |
| Smart Export Guarantee scope | Installations up to 5 MW (or up to 50 kW for micro-CHP) of solar PV, wind, micro-CHP, hydro or anaerobic digestion, located in Great Britain. Export meter readings are mandatory. SEG Licensees set the rate, contract length and other terms; the rate must always be above zero | MW; kW; p/kWh (unspecified) | GB, current as of 2026. | Ofgem, 'Smart Export Guarantee (SEG)', https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/environmental-and-social-schemes/smart-export-guarantee-seg | No floor price beyond 'above zero' and no published tariff range on the Ofgem page. Storage eligibility and the treatment of grid-charged exported electricity are NOT addressed on that page and must be confirmed with the specific SEG licensee. |
| IPMVP options and current version | Option A and B: retrofit isolation (measurement boundary around the affected equipment). Option C: whole facility (uses the meters measuring total facility supply). Option D: calibrated simulation (where baseline data are unreliable or unavailable). Current version: IPMVP Core Concepts 2022 | n/a (method) | International standard for M&V of energy savings. Core principle: savings cannot be directly measured because they represent the absence of consumption, so they are determined by comparing measured consumption before and after with adjustments for changed conditions. | Efficiency Valuation Organization, 'International Performance Measurement and Verification Protocol (IPMVP) Core Concepts 2022', https://evo-world.org/en/products-services-mainmenu-en/protocols/ipmvp | The public page does not define 'adjusted baseline', routine or non-routine adjustments; the full protocol is required. Option C is the natural fit for a bill-share on a single-meter C&I site. |
| Shared-savings contract characteristics for BTM storage | Third-party ownership with the provider financing and owning the asset on the host's site; the provider receives a percentage of energy savings and revenue generated; contract typically ten or more years; host bears no capital, maintenance or operational burden | years; % share (unspecified) | North American C&I market, vendor description. Cited case: one facility 'has realized $495,742 in energy cost savings to date'. | Peak Power, 'Energy Storage Shared Savings Agreement', https://peakpowerenergy.com/2023/11/15/energy-storage-shared-savings-agreement/ | VENDOR MARKETING SOURCE — the lowest tier of evidence. Percentage splits are not disclosed. Use only for structural characteristics, not for economics; corroborate against the Billimoria and Simshauser taxonomy for a peer-reviewed framing. |
| Storage contract taxonomy and typical tenor | Four classifying axes: derivative index (net periodic revenue; yardstick net periodic revenue: optimal or spread); contractual form (swap; options — put floor, call cap, collar; grants or service contracts; availability-scaled); periodicity (daily, monthly, quarterly, yearly); tenor. Exchange-traded contracts have historically had maximum tenors of single years, while bilateral and negotiated contracts 'tend to be structured over a longer periods (i.e. 7 to 15 years) to underwrite project financings' | years; taxonomy | Peer-reviewed working paper; quantitative case study is on South Australia in the NEM, but the taxonomy and the incentive-compatibility argument are general. | Billimoria, F., Simshauser, P., 'Contract design for storage in hybrid electricity markets', EPRG Working Paper 2304 / Cambridge Working Paper in Economics 2322, February 2023, §2.1 and Figure 2, https://www.jbs.cam.ac.uk/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/eprg-wp2304.pdf | Authors state 'standard contract forms are yet to emerge' and find that 'preferences for structural simplicity in contract design could introduce incentive incompatibility'. Not GB-specific and not BTM-specific. |
| GB balancing services stacking principle (legacy statement) | 'Revenues from different system services can be cumulated as long as tendered availability windows do not overlap' | n/a (rule) | GB, September 2017, when National Grid procured over 20 different balancing services products. | Charles River Associates, 'An introduction: Revenue streams for battery storage', September 2017, https://media.crai.com/sites/default/files/publications/DEL_GB%20Battery%20Revenue%20Stacking.pdf | SUPERSEDED IN DETAIL — the product set has since been replaced by DC/DM/DR with an explicit prohibition on stacking two DFR services even in non-overlapping form within an EFA block. Cited only as the historical articulation of the non-overlap principle; do not use its product list or prices. |
| MHHS status | Over 50% of industry MPANs migrated; milestones M10 (central systems ready), M11/M12 (migration commenced), M13 (Load Shaping System live) achieved; M15 (full migration) and M16 (cutover to the new Settlement Timetable) outstanding | % of MPANs; milestone identifiers | GB market-wide half hourly settlement programme, status as reported on the programme website. | MHHS Programme, https://www.mhhsprogramme.co.uk/ | No calendar dates were given on the page for M15/M16. DNO charging statements already reflect MHHS: LPN's 2026/27 statement distinguishes Migrated from Non-Migrated MPANs and applies different residual band allocation rules (by consumption for aggregated/transitional-protection MPANs, by MIC for site-specific). |

## GB Balancing Mechanism, skip rates, and dispatch selection — as a revenue stream for behind-the-meter C&I lithium-ion BESS (0.1–5 MW, 1–4 h), covering BM Unit submission mechanics (PN, MEL/MIL, BOD, dynamic parameters), NESO dispatch selection (Open Balancing Platform, Bulk Dispatch, National Dispatch Optimiser, Balancing Reserve), the causes and measured magnitude of skips, BM access routes for BTM assets (Wider Access, VLP/Secondary BMU, P344/P375/P415), aggregator route-to-market economics, and imbalance/cash-out exposure.

| Constant | Value | Units | Basis / conditions | Source | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Skip rate definition (formula) | Skip Rate = Total volume of skipped in-merit bid/offer ÷ Total volume of in-merit bid/offer | dimensionless (%) | GB Balancing Mechanism. In-merit volume determined at 5-minute granularity by reconstructing a price-ordered stack sized to the actual balancing energy requirement of that 5-minute period. Monthly/annual figures are volume-weighted averages of the 5-minute rates, weighted by the volume requirement. | LCP Delta for NESO, 'BM Skip Rates Phase 2 Report', November 2024; also NESO/LCP Delta 'Enhancing Storage in the Balancing Mechanism', 7 Nov 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/348241/download | Phase 1 methodology (compare to marginal accepted price) gives materially higher numbers than Phase 2 (reconstructed stack). Never mix the two. |
| NESO definition of a skip | A skip occurs when a non-economic dispatch decision is made: if NESO sends a BOA at a higher price than an alternative action that could have been taken, the alternative action is skipped | n/a (definition) | GB, official NESO definition adopted December 2024 after LCP Delta engagement. | NESO, 'Defining, measuring, and addressing skip rates', December 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/348236/download | NESO explicitly notes some skips reflect legitimate operational constraints and 'do not necessarily represent inefficient dispatch'. |

| Grid Code dynamic parameter set (BC1.A.1.5) | Up to three Run-Up Rates and three Run-Down Rates (MW/minute) with Run-Up/Run-Down Elbows (MW); Notice to Deviate from Zero (NDZ, minutes); Notice to Deliver Offers (NTO) and Notice to Deliver Bids (NTB, minutes, max 2 minutes for a Genset); Minimum Zero Time (MZT, minutes); Minimum Non-Zero Time (MNZT, minutes); Stable Export Limit (SEL, MW); Stable Import Limit (SIL, MW); Maximum Delivery Volume (MDV, MWh) over a Maximum Delivery Period (MDP, minutes) | MW, MW/minute, MWh, minutes | GB, all BM Units. SEL and SIL are expressed at the Grid Entry Point or Grid Supply Point as appropriate. | NESO, Grid Code Balancing Code No.1, BC1.A.1.5 Dynamic Parameters, https://www.neso.energy/document/36456/download and BC1 Issue 6 Revision 22, 2 April 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/33851/download | Ofgem's September 2020 open letter requires dynamic parameters to reflect true operating characteristics and prohibits their use for commercial gain. |
| LCP Delta exclusion thresholds (Stage 2 and Stage 5) | Stage 2: exclude MZT ≥ 12 hours, MNZT ≥ 12 hours, NDZ ≥ 90 minutes (with zero post-BOA output at end of previous 5-minute period, unless already running or holding a warming contract); cap volumes at SEL/SIL. Stage 5: exclude actions that would sync a unit with MNZT or NDZ ≥ 31 minutes, or desync a unit with MZT ≥ 31 minutes | hours; minutes | GB, applied to all BM Units in the skip rate calculation. | LCP Delta for NESO, 'BM Skip Rates Phase 2 Report', November 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/348241/download | LCP note gate closure of 60 minutes from the end of the current settlement period means the earliest an instruction can be sent is 89 minutes prior to delivery. |
| Bid-Offer Data structure and gate closure | Up to 10 Bid-Offer Pairs per BM Unit per Settlement Period; no changes to Physical Notification or Bid-Offer Data may be submitted after Gate Closure; gate closure is 60 minutes; the BM Window is 90 minutes | count; minutes | GB. Bid-Offer Data may not be submitted unless an automatic logging device is installed at the Control Point (CC.6.5.8(b)/ECC.6.5.8(b)). Other Relevant Data due by 11:00 each day. | NESO, Grid Code BC1 Appendix 1 (BC1.A.1.4) and BC1.4.4, Issue 6 Revision 22, 2 April 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/33851/download; gate closure 60 min per LCP Delta 'BM Skip Rates Phase 2 Report' 2024; BM Window 90 min per Ofgem GC0166 decision 2025 | Pairs are conventionally numbered 1..5 (offers) and −1..−5 (bids). LCP Delta's Phase 2 change to consider all pairs, not just the first, materially changed measured skip rates. |
| Minimum BM Unit size and aggregation scope (Wider Access) | 1 MW minimum BM Unit size; aggregation permitted only within Grid Supply Point Groups (DNO areas) | MW | GB, applies identically to the Supplier Additional BMU (ABMU) route and the Virtual Lead Party Secondary BMU (SBMU) route. | NESO, 'Wider Access to the Balancing Mechanism: Onboarding and Registration Guidance for Suppliers and Virtual Lead Parties', 22 April 2026, https://www.neso.energy/document/150276/download | Binding constraint for BTM assets below 1 MW, which cannot access the BM standalone. |
| Wider Access registration costs and lead times | Supplier Agreement or VLP Agreement £5,000 plus VAT; end-to-end registration up to 5 months; NESO BM System SORT upload occurs once every 8 weeks; SMP application one week prior to SORT cut-off; operational metering testing booked 4 weeks prior to SORT cut-off | £; months; weeks | GB, Supplier and VLP routes. EDL, EDT and control/system telephony required; EIC code from the NESO Local Issuing Office required before BMU entry into NESO systems; new BSC Parties may need Elexon Performance Assurance Board approval. | NESO, 'Wider Access to the Balancing Mechanism' guidance, 22 April 2026, https://www.neso.energy/document/150276/download | Excludes Elexon registration costs and third-party EDL/EDT software costs, which are additional. |
| BM operational metering standards | BAU standard: readings every second with accuracy ±1%. Small Aggregated Asset (SAA) derogation: ±2.5% accuracy and 60-second reads, capped at 300 MW total, individual asset capacity below 1 MW | seconds; % accuracy; MW cap | GB. SAA derogation followed the Power Responsive trial and requires a Supplier or VLP SAA Metering Capacity Agreement. | NESO, 'Wider Access to the Balancing Mechanism' guidance, 22 April 2026, https://www.neso.energy/document/150276/download | The 300 MW SAA cohort is capped; availability must be confirmed with NESO's Power Responsive team. |
| Aggregator Impact Matrix requirement | For each Additional BM Unit and Secondary BM Unit: data on the effect of a Bid-Offer Acceptance on each Grid Supply Point within the GSP Group (% impact per GSP), plus the postcodes and MSIDs that make up the unit | % per GSP; identifiers | GB, mandatory for aggregated (Additional and Secondary) BM Units. | NESO, Grid Code BC1.A.10 Aggregator Impact Matrix, Issue 6 Revision 22, 2 April 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/33851/download | A data prerequisite for a BTM asset entering the BM via an aggregator; MSID-level disclosure is required. |
| P375 asset metering behind the boundary point | Approved 24 February 2021, implementation date 30 June 2022; created Code of Practice 11 and Asset Metering System Identifiers (AMSIDs) registered with the SVAA | date; identifier scheme | GB. Proposed by Flexitricity 13 December 2018. Allows secondary meters behind the boundary point to be used for settlement of bid-offer acceptances so that only activity related to the asset is settled. | Ofgem, 'BSC P375: Settlement of Secondary BM Units using metering behind the site Boundary Point', decision 24 February 2021, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/docs/2021/02/p375_final_authority_decision.pdf | Elexon's Asset Metering guidance requires Asset Metering Systems to sit behind a Half Hourly Boundary Point Metering System. AMVLP is an Elexon role; a separate VLP process with NESO is required for BM access. |
| P415 wholesale market access for VLP-dispatched flexibility | Ofgem decision published 6 October 2023; implementation date 7 November 2024 | date | GB. Raised by Enel X UK on 30 September 2020. Introduces Deviation Volumes; supplier compensation priced using Ofgem's Price Cap Methodology and mutualised across suppliers under the directed Proposed Solution. | Ofgem, 'Decision: BSC P415 Facilitating access to wholesale markets for flexibility dispatched by Virtual Lead Parties', 6 October 2023, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/publications/ofgem-decision-p415-facilitating-access-wholesale-markets-flexibility-dispatched-vlps | NESO states a Virtual Trading Party role alone cannot participate in the BM; VLP registration is also required. |
| P344 (Wider Access / TERRE) origin of the VLP route | Approved by Ofgem 24 August 2018; NESO enabled Wider Access to the BM as a new route to market in 2019 | date | GB. | Growth in BM Units | More than 500 new units registered to operate within the BM since April 2021, a 55% overall increase of assets on the system | count; % | GB, April 2021 to December 2024. | NESO, 'Defining, measuring, and addressing skip rates', December 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/348236/download | NESO notes that historically 'just 5% of the market' required rebalancing. |

Registration on the Single Market Platform required. From October 2025, afternoon auctions co-optimise Balancing Reserve with Quick Reserve and Dynamic Response Services. | NESO, 'Balancing Reserve' service page, accessed August 2026, https://www.neso.energy/industry-information/balancing-services/reserve-services/balancing-reserve | NESO does not publish the MW requirement on this page; requirements are set so the risk of loss of load due to reserve shortfall is uniform across settlement periods. |

In-merit dispatch rate 18% (up from 11% in September); dispatched battery volume 227 GWh, +28% month-on-month. | Modo Energy, 'ME BESS GB: Revenues climb to £77k/MW/year in October 2025', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/battery-energy-storage-revenues-gb-october-2025-record-balancing-mechanism-dispatch-rates | 'In-merit dispatch rate' is Modo's metric and is not identical to (1 − NESO skip rate). |

| Cash-out reform parameters (EBSCR) | PAR stepped from 250 MWh (early winter 2014/15) to 50 MWh (early winter 2015/16) to 1 MWh (early winter 2018/19); move from dual to single cash-out price; Reserve Scarcity Pricing with Value of Lost Load at £3,000/MWh before winter 2018/19 rising to £6,000/MWh thereafter | MWh; £/MWh | GB imbalance settlement. Single price is paid or received by all parties and reflects balancing costs avoided. | Ofgem, 'Electricity Balancing Significant Code Review – Final Policy Decision Impact Assessment', May 2014, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/docs/2014/05/electricity_balancing_significant_code_review_-_final_policy_decision_impact_assessment_1.pdf | 2014 policy decision; confirm current PAR and VoLL against Elexon's live Imbalance Pricing Guidance before use, as subsequent modifications may have adjusted parameters. |
| P462 (removal of subsidies from Bid Prices in the BM) | Claimed consumer saving of £518 million by 2030; current subsidised wind bids sometimes reach −£100/MWh or lower; effect would move charging prices from deeply negative to around £0/MWh | £ million; £/MWh | GB. Raised by NESO in October 2023. Timera cite wind bidding at −£60/MWh with ROCs or lower for CfD units. Modo/NESO roadmap places the Assessment Report at February 2026 and the Final Modification Report at April 2026. | Modo Energy, 'P462: Could fixing a market distortion cause another?', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/p462-gb-balancing-mechanism-reform-wind-battery-energy-storage-cfd-subsidy-pricing-distortion; Timera Energy, 'Key changes are impacting BESS constraint revenue capture', https://timera-energy.com/blog/key-changes-are-impacting-bess-constraint-revenue-capture/ | Industry analytics and consultancy blog sources; the £518m figure is the proposer's claim. Not yet implemented at the time of this survey. |
| NESO reform milestone dates relevant to skip rates | OBP constraint management feature early 2025; OBP national optimisation and constraint optimisation summer 2025; OBP–NCMS interface early 2026; National Dispatch Optimiser parallel run January 2026; DC/DM/DR frequency response within OBP from January 2026; skip rate reduction target confirmed February 2026; Root Cause Analysis findings April 2026; optimisation within a constraint April 2026; GC0166 final switch June 2026; FSoE model in NDO June 2026; GC0166 full implementation early November 2026 | date | GB. NESO's stated goal is a substantial reduction in avoidable skips by the end of Business Plan 3 (March 2026) under RIIO-2. | NESO, 'Defining, measuring, and addressing skip rates', December 2024, https://www.neso.energy/document/348236/download; Modo Energy, 'NESO Roadmap 2026', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/gb-neso-national-energy-system-operator-2026-energy-storage-roadmap-p462-connections-reform-balancing-mechanism | Committed dates, subject to slippage. NESO has stated 'the low hanging fruit has already been picked'. |

Files: Skip Rate - Summary (All-BM and PSA by stage, 30-min); In Merit All Balancing Mechanism (monthly, BMU-level); In Merit Post System Action (monthly, BMU-level, fields date, bm_unit, fuel, bid_offer, stage, available_volume_MWh, average_price_per_MWh, pair_id, accepted_volume_MWh, in_merit_volume_MWh, skipped_volume_MWh); Exclusion Reasons (monthly); Stage 5 PSA Skip Rate by Technology; Stage 6 PSA Skip Rate; Stage 6 Acceptance (5-min prices and volumes); DSF-specific files. | NESO Data Portal, 'Skip Rates' dataset, https://www.neso.energy/data-portal/skip-rates and https://api.neso.energy/api/3/action/package_show?id=skip-rates | Data was updated 18 November 2025 to correct a long-notice unit exclusion bug affecting consistency. No published breakdown by region, price band or GSP group — these must be derived by joining to Elexon BM Unit reference data. |

NTO/NTB + ramping); NTO/NTB ≤2 min; from zero FPN: SEL ≤25 MW and MNZT ≤5 min (positive BR) / SIL ≤25 MW and MZT ≤5 min (negative BR); from non-zero FPN: min instruction 1 MW, min duration 1 minute | MW; minutes | GB; BM Units only, with backup control/system telephony during contracted windows; dispatched by BOA | NESO, 'Balancing Reserve Guidance Document', Version 7, June 2026, pp.8-10, https://www.neso.energy/document/303546/download | Single clearing price in £/MW/hour per service window paid to all successful providers. The unit must be able to deliver full contracted capacity per Service Window — this reserves energy that cannot also be sold elsewhere. |

Elexon publishes NIV and system prices ~1 hour after the settlement period and re-adjusts up to 6 months later. |

BCLP explicitly notes 'there is no standard approach to these optimisation agreements in the GB market' and gives no percentages of its own. |
| Modo index modelling efficiencies | Import efficiency 85%, export efficiency 99% (applied to non-BMU assets) | % | GB; used when modelling non-BMU behaviour to reflect physical limitations; index assets must be ≥6 MW | Modo Energy, 'GB Methodology — Indices', https://modoenergy.com/methodology/gb | Modo's methodology explicitly excludes degradation. Peer-reviewed GB BM modelling (Nosratabadi et al. 2024) uses 85% round-trip for Li-ion and 79% for pumped storage. |
| Locational marginal BM value of storage (peer-reviewed) | 1 MW / 2h Li-ion: monthly BM cost reduction up to ~£3,800–4,000 (best GSP group, summer). 100 MW / 2h: January monthly BM cost reduction ~£15,500 (HES, worst) to ~£372,000 (PSH in GSP group H, best) | £/month | GB; 1,982-node transmission model coupled to a BM redispatch optimisation; January and July 2022; 14 GSP groups; all balancing units above 1 MW included | Nosratabadi, S.M. et al., 'The Impact of Grid Storage on Balancing Costs and Carbon Emissions in Great Britain', arXiv:2410.07740, 2024, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.07740 | This is system value (BM cost saved), not revenue to the asset. The paper concludes that at 2022–2024 capex, 1 MW storage NPVs are negative on BM+DC+CM alone in every GSP group — 'balancing services alone are presently insufficient for financial viability'. |
| eGAMA availability submission thresholds (embedded) | ≥10 MW in North Scotland (SHET); ≥30 MW in South Scotland (SPT); ≥100 MW in England and Wales (NGET) | MW | GB; embedded generation availability reporting | NESO, 'Registration guide: Balancing Mechanism Units (BMUs)', June 2026, p.7, https://www.neso.energy/document/290631/download | 0.1–5 MW BTM assets fall below all three thresholds, so eGAMA submission is not required — but this also means NESO has no independent availability view of the asset outside MEL/MIL/MDO/MDB. |

'Frequency' is auto-assigned when an action reaches target in <5 min, holds <5 min, and the absolute change is >30 MW. |
| BSUoS charging basis | Final Demand only from 1 April 2023; ex-ante fixed volumetric tariff published for 6 months with 9 months' notice | — | GB; 'Final Demand' = electricity consumed other than for the purposes of generation or export onto the network | Ofgem/NGESO CMP308 and CMP361 (WACM3) decisions; NESO, 'BSUoS fixed tariff model methodology', https://www.neso.energy/document/262041/download | Determines whether a BTM battery's charging energy attracts BSUoS. The apportionment between site final demand and generation/export-purposed consumption is site-specific and should be confirmed with the supplier/DNO. |

| Non-delivery penalty rate and caps | Penalty rate PR = PE / 24, i.e. the CPI-indexed clearing price in £/MW/year divided by 24, expressed in £/MWh of shortfall. Monthly penalty cap = 200% of that month's capacity payment. Annual penalty cap = 100% of annual capacity payments. | £/MWh; % of monthly capacity payment; % of annual capacity payment | Great Britain, all auctions to date. | The Electricity Capacity Regulations 2014 (SI 2014/2043), Schedule 1 paragraphs 5(3) and 6(4); regulation 41(3A); NESO Capacity Market Auction Static Data (Annual Penalty Cap 100%, Monthly Penalty Cap 200% for every auction from 2016-17 TR to 2029-30 T-4) | At the 2029/30 T-4 price of £27,100/MW/yr this gives PR = £1,129/MWh. Because a settlement period is 0.5h, a 1 MW shortfall for one settlement period costs about £565. The annual cap means the worst-case annual outcome is losing the capacity payment plus paying an equal amount, not an unbounded loss. |

Provision deadline 15 working days after the conditional prequalification notice. | £ per MW of de-rated capacity | Great Britain, T-4 and T-1 auctions. | The Electricity Capacity Regulations 2014 (SI 2014/2043), regulation 59, https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/2043/regulation/59 | Per MW de-rated, so a 2 MW / 4 MWh BTM battery with a 0.44 MW obligation posts roughly £4,400 at £10,000/MW — small in absolute terms but drawn down on non-delivery. |

It does not mean the obligation is costless: the 4-hour CMN response requirement still constrains state of charge. |

| Minimum capacity threshold for prequalification | 1 MW | MW of connection capacity (generating/interconnector CMU) or DSR capacity (DSR CMU) | Great Britain, all CMU types. | The Electricity Capacity Regulations 2014 (SI 2014/2043), regulation 15, https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/2043/regulation/15 | Applies to connection/DSR capacity, not de-rated capacity. | Technology Class Weighted Average Availability (TCWAA) for battery storage, inferred from the de-rating curve plateau | 0.9216 (2026/27 T-1 and 2029/30 T-4); 0.9299 (2028/29 T-4); 0.9552 (2019/20 T-1); 0.9508 (2020/21 T-1); 0.9464 (2021/22 T-1) | fraction | Great Britain. The plateau value of the published EFC curve equals the TCWAA, because the raw EFC tends to 1 for long durations and the Schedule 3B methodology multiplies by TCWAA. | Derived from NESO, Capacity Market De-Rating Factors dataset, read together with DESNZ/NESO Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, Schedule 3B ('the outputs of the model for each Storage Generating Technology Class shall be multiplied by the Technology Class Weighted Average Availability') and Rule 13.4A.4(a) | INFERRED, not directly published as a TCWAA figure. This matters because Adjusted Connection Capacity for the Extended Performance Test is Connection Capacity x TCWAA. Verify against the relevant Auction Guidelines before relying on it for an EPT feasibility constraint. |
| Satisfactory Performance Day requirements | 3 SPDs during Winter (1 October - 30 April), of which at least 1 between 1 January and 30 April; each SPD requires capacity at or above the Capacity Obligation for at least one Settlement Period (30 minutes). On failure: 3 further SPDs between 1 May and 31 July, and capacity payments suspended from 1 May. Doubled to 6 SPDs if the CMU fails to deliver at least 1 kWh in a stress event in each of two or more months in which stress events occurred. | count of days; count of settlement periods; date windows | Great Britain, every Capacity Committed CMU, every Delivery Year. | DESNZ/NESO, Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, Rules 13.4.1, 13.4.1ZA, 13.4.6 | Declared Long Stop CMUs are exempt in the first (or, for a Declared 24 Month Long Stop CMU, second) Delivery Year. |
| Extended Performance Test frequency and threshold | At least one Satisfactory Performance Day in the Winter of the first Delivery Year, and thereafter at least once in the Winter of every third Capacity Year. Threshold: output at or above Adjusted Connection Capacity (= Connection Capacity x TCWAA) for consecutive Settlement Periods equal in duration to the declared class's minimum duration. | years; MW; consecutive settlement periods | Great Britain, CMUs in a Storage Generating Technology Class and Unproven DSR CMUs with a declared Storage Facility on multi-year agreements. | DESNZ/NESO, Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, Rules 13.4A.2, 13.4A.3, 13.4A.4 | For a 2h-class battery with CC = 2 MW and TCWAA = 0.9216, the test requires 1.843 MW for 4 consecutive settlement periods, i.e. 3.69 MWh delivered at the meter, at years 1, 4, 7, 10 and 13 of a 15-year agreement, after degradation. |
| Auction and prequalification timetable (indicative) | T-31 weeks Prequalification Window opens; T-23 weeks closes; T-17 weeks Prequalification Results Day and Initial Dispute Window opens; T-16 weeks dispute window closes; T-13 weeks dispute decisions; T-3 weeks updated Auction Parameters; T-2 weeks Price-Maker and Rule 5.5.11/5.5.13 decisions; T first Bidding Window | weeks before the auction | Great Britain, all Capacity Auctions, unless the Delivery Body or Secretary of State determines otherwise. | DESNZ/NESO, Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, Rule 2.2.2 | Auctions are typically held in February/March. Prequalification therefore opens around the preceding July/August. |
| Proven DSR Capacity derivation | Twice the lowest of three evidenced DSR Volumes, from three separate Settlement Periods within the 2 years before the start of the Prequalification Window, in each of which a Baseline Demand could be calculated and a positive DSR Volume was delivered in discharge of a balancing services obligation | MW (from MWh per half hour) | Great Britain, Proven DSR CMUs. | DESNZ/NESO, Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, Rule 13.2.6(a)(i)-(iii) | Requires a DNO letter confirming the Line Loss Factor methodology and values for the three periods. Effectively impossible for a greenfield BTM battery, forcing the Unproven DSR route with credit cover. |
| Capacity Market monthly payment formula | ACP_ix = CO_ix x PE_ix; MCP_im = ACP_ix x WF_mx; PE_ix (T-4) = CCP_i x (CPI_x / CPI_base); PE_ix (T-1) = CCP_i | £; £/MW/year; dimensionless weighting factor summing to 1 across the capacity year | Great Britain. WF_mx is the share of GB electrical demand (GWh) in the corresponding month over a rolling 3-year calculation period, computed to 3 decimal places at least 3 months before the capacity year. | The Electricity Capacity Regulations 2014 (SI 2014/2043), Schedule 1 paragraphs 2 and 3, https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/2043/schedule/1 | Actual WF values are published annually by EMR Settlement Ltd per delivery year; the EMRS pages returned HTTP 403 to automated retrieval, so the numeric monthly values were not captured here and must be sourced directly. |
| Delivery Year, Capacity Year and Winter definitions | Capacity Year: 1 October to the following 30 September. Winter: 1 October to the following 30 April. Core Winter Period: 1 December to the last day of the following February. | date ranges | Great Britain. | The Electricity Capacity Regulations 2014 (SI 2014/2043), regulation 2; DESNZ/NESO Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, definition of 'Core Winter Period' | The CM year is offset from both the calendar year and the UK financial year. A model that aligns CM revenue to a 1 April or 1 January year start will misallocate roughly two quarters of revenue and mis-time every SPD and EPT constraint. |
| Duration Limited trigger for a Storage Generating Technology Class | At least 5% of Loss of Load Occurrences for the corresponding Delivery Year estimated by the Delivery Body to last longer than the class's specified minimum duration | % of Loss of Load Occurrences | Great Britain, determined per auction; the Auction Guidelines must state which classes are Duration Limited (Rule 2.3.4C). | DESNZ/NESO, Capacity Market Rules consolidation 24 July 2026, Rule 2.3.4B | If a class is NOT Duration Limited, Rule 2.3.4(a) applies the TCWAA instead of the EFC — which explains the 0.9611 value for 2h storage in the 2021-22 T-4 and the plateau in every published curve. |

## GB frequency response markets — Dynamic Containment, Dynamic Moderation and Dynamic Regulation (all six directions DCL/DCH/DML/DMH/DRL/DRH), plus the legacy and adjacent services (Static FFR, Dynamic FFR, EFR, MFR, Balancing Reserve, Quick Reserve, Slow Reserve, STOR), scoped for a 0.1–5 MW behind-the-meter C&I lithium-ion BESS in Great Britain that must be optimised half-hourly and underwritten to lender standard.

| Constant | Value | Units | Basis / conditions | Source | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Deadband (all six products) | ±0.015 Hz from 50.000 Hz (i.e. 49.985–50.015 Hz); no response delivered and no baseline deviation permitted inside it | Hz | GB, current service terms v6 effective 31 July 2026; aligned with Article 154, Annex 5 of the System Operator Guidelines as retained EU law | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, effective 31 July 2026, Schedule 2 service parameter table (fd), https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | A 'Zero Deadband' (fd = 0) may be designated, but only for Auction Units that are NOT Energy Limited — so not for a typical BESS providing these services. |
| Dynamic Containment delivery curve | 0 % inside deadband; linear to 5 % at ±0.2 Hz (knee); linear to 100 % at ±0.5 Hz (saturation); 2.30 % at ±0.1 Hz | % of contracted quantity vs frequency deviation | GB, v6 effective 31 July 2026 | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 Table 1 and service parameter table, https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | Linear interpolation between the eight published points; beyond ±0.5 Hz the response is held at the saturation value. |
| Dynamic Moderation delivery curve | 0 % inside deadband; linear to 5 % at ±0.1 Hz (knee); linear to 100 % at ±0.2 Hz (saturation) | % of contracted quantity vs frequency deviation | GB, v6 effective 31 July 2026 | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 Table 1, https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | — |
| Dynamic Regulation delivery curve | 0 % inside deadband; linear from ±0.015 Hz to 100 % at ±0.2 Hz; no knee point; 45.95 % at ±0.1 Hz | % of contracted quantity vs frequency deviation | GB, v6 effective 31 July 2026 | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 Table 1, https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | — |
| Maximum initiation time (TiMAX) | DC 0.5 s; DM 0.5 s; DR 2 s (0.5 s when stacked with DC and/or DM) | seconds | GB, v6 effective 31 July 2026 | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 service parameters, https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | — |
| Maximum time to full delivery (TdMAX) | DC 1 s; DM 1 s; DR 10 s (1 s when stacked with DC and/or DM) | seconds | GB, v6 effective 31 July 2026 | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 service parameters; corroborated by NESO, 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance', June 2026, Table 1, https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | — |
| Delivery duration (Tsus) | DC 15 minutes; DM 30 minutes; DR 60 minutes | minutes of sustained delivery at contracted quantity | GB, v6 effective 31 July 2026; applies to energy-limited providers | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 service parameters, https://www.neso.energy/document/384606/download | — |
| Contracted Response Energy Volume (REV) | REV = (Tsus/60) × Q: DC 0.25 × Q MWh; DM 0.5 × Q MWh; DR 1.0 × Q MWh | MWh per MW contracted | GB; stacked contracts sum their REVs | NESO, 'Response Services Service Terms', Version 6, Schedule 2 (VmaxC); NESO, 'SOE Monitoring Guidance', V2, March 2025, Table 2, https://www.neso.energy/document/347241/download | Explicitly NOT the maximum energy that could be delivered over a contracted service period — it is the threshold below which SoE management becomes necessary to avoid unavailability. |

Solution 3: DC-coupled battery and load on one inverter, DC meters on both, loss adjustment on both. AC-coupled sites may use Solutions 1 or 2 (BMU/BMU, NBMU/BMU or NBMU/NBMU); DC-coupled sites may only use Solution 3 (BMU/BMU or NBMU/NBMU only — mixed NBMU/BMU is not compatible). The previous wording — "entirely unavoidable by dispatch; a BTM battery cannot reduce this" — was WRONG, and it was wrong in the direction that costs money.** The band is allocated on AGREED CAPACITY, not on consumption, so a battery that lowers the site's worst-case peak lets the customer hold a lower agreed capacity and drop a band. The steps are large: LV4→LV3 is £23.798729/day = **£8,692/yr**, LV2→LV1 £5.714442/day = **£2,087/yr**. Two conditions, both real: the customer must apply to the DNO to reduce the agreed capacity (a customer action, so the stream is CONDITIONAL and stays out of the priced floor), and re-banding follows DCUSA Schedule 32 on a 24-month average MIC basis, so it cannot land in year 1. The original clause that IS still right: increasing MIC increases this via a band step. Priced by `MarketStream.AGREED_CAPACITY_REDUCTION`. |

GB calculation applies the rate to 100% of electricity supplied to non-EIIs and 0% to EIIs. | DESNZ, Calculating the level of the Renewables Obligation for 2026 to 2027, 29 September 2025, https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/renewables-obligation-level-calculations-2026-to-2027/calculating-the-level-of-the-renewables-obligation-for-2026-to-2027; Ofgem, Renewables Obligation: buy-out price and mutualisation threshold and ceilings 2026/27, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/data/renewables-obligation-buy-out-price-and-mutualisation-threshold-and-ceilings-2026-2027 | Indexation moved from RPI to CPI on 1 April 2026 (government decision 28 January 2026); the £69.34 reflects 3.4% CPI for calendar 2025. Actual supplier cost is the ROC market price, which trades at buy-out plus recycle value, so the true pass-through exceeds £32.73/MWh. The £32.73/MWh figure is a derived product of two sourced numbers, not itself a published rate. |
| CfD supplier obligation — Interim Levy Rate | Approximately £10/MWh in Q1 2026; forecast up to £20.67/MWh by Q3 2027 | £/MWh | GB, quarterly obligation periods; ILR is a pre-payment 'charged at a daily £/MWh rate to fund the cost of CfD generation payments', plus a Total Reserve Amount | Energy Advice Hub, A guide to non-commodity cost rises on your electricity bill 2026 and 2027 (industry explainer — second-tier source), https://energyadvicehub.org/a-guide-to-non-commodity-cost-rises-on-your-electricity-bill-2026-and-2027/; mechanism per LCCC Data Portal, Forecast ILR TRA, https://dp.lowcarboncontracts.uk/dataset/forecast-ilr-tra | Second-tier source for the numeric value; the authoritative quarterly determinations are published by LCCC and should be re-fetched per quarter. ILR moves inversely with wholesale price, so it must be modelled as negatively correlated with arbitrage revenue, not as an independent variable. |
| CfD, Capacity Market and Nuclear RAB operational costs levies from 1 April 2026 | CfD operational costs levy: £0.1463/MWh (2026-27), £0.1714/MWh (2027-28), £0.1900/MWh (2028 onwards). Capacity Market settlement costs levy: £9,112,000 (2026-27), £10,346,000 (2027-28), £11,474,000 (2028 onwards) total. Nuclear RAB operational costs levy: £0.0063/MWh (2026-27), £0.0068/MWh (2027-28), £0.0073/MWh (2028 onwards) | £/MWh; £ total per year | England, Wales, Scotland (and Northern Ireland for CfD/RAB); administration levies only | The Electricity Supplier Payments (Amendment) Regulations 2026, effective 1 April 2026, https://www.legislationtracker.co.uk/article/the-electricity-supplier-payments-amendment-regulations-2026-25-03-26 | These are ADMINISTRATION levies and are two to three orders of magnitude smaller than the main CfD supplier obligation (~£10/MWh), the CM supplier charge, and the Nuclear RAB revenue levy (~£3.5–4.7/MWh). Do not confuse them. |
| Nuclear RAB (Sizewell C) revenue levy | £3.494/MWh (December 2025); £3.663/MWh (Q1 2026); £4.683/MWh (Q2 2026); industry forecast range £3.50–£4.50/MWh for 2026–2027 | £/MWh | GB, collected by suppliers under quarterly rates set by LCCC (ILR plus Total Reserve Amount plus operational costs levy), first charged from 1 December 2025 | Utility Bidder, Nuclear RAB Levy: what UK businesses need to know in 2026, 15 April 2026 (industry explainer — second-tier source), https://www.utilitybidder.co.uk/business-electricity/nuclear-rab-levy-uk-business-energy-bills/ | Second-tier source; verify against LCCC's quarterly determinations. EII certificate holders are exempt. Any cost stack calibrated on a bill dated before December 2025 will omit this line entirely. |
| AAHEDC tariff | 0.044201 p/kWh forecast for 2026/27 (Shetland component 0.012766 p/kWh; AAHEDC excluding Shetland Assistance Amount 0.031435 p/kWh); +7.8% on 2025/26 | p/kWh | GB, recovered from all authorised Suppliers under Condition F8 of the NESO Electricity System Operator Licence. Based on a forecast demand base of 273.7 TWh and Total Scheme Amount of £121m (Assistance £85.0m, Shetland Assistance £34.9m, Administration £0.16m, Correction £0.8m). | NESO, Assistance for Areas with High Electricity Distribution Costs forecast tariff 2026/27, 27 March 2026, https://www.neso.energy/sites/neso.energy/files/documents/Draft%202026-27%20AAHEDC%20Tariff.pdf | Forecast tariff. Final tariff published mid-July but effective retrospectively from 1 April, invoiced quarterly in arrears — model a true-up accrual. |
| EII Support Levy / Network Charging Compensation scheme uplift | NCC relief rises from 60% to 90% on 1 April 2026; estimated impact £1–£2/year per household and £0.40/MWh for non-domestic consumers from this uplift alone; EII Support Levy to increase again from 1 April 2027 with a 6-month forecast to suppliers in October 2026 | % relief; £/MWh | GB, from 1 April 2026 | DESNZ, Proposed uplift to the Network Charging Compensation scheme for energy intensive industries (EIIs): government consultation response, https://www.gov.uk/government/consultations/network-charging-compensation-scheme-uplift-for-energy-intensive-industries/outcome/proposed-uplift-to-the-network-charging-compensation-scheme-for-energy-intensive-industries-eiis-government-consultation-response | The consultation response does not give the total ESL £/MWh rate, only the incremental £0.40/MWh from the uplift. A non-EII C&I site pays this; an EII-certified site receives 90% relief on its own network charges instead. |
| Capacity Market supplier charge — statutory basis | Peak period ('periods of high demand') = 4 p.m. to 7 p.m. on any working day in November, December, January or February. Relevant demand = 'the amount of electricity (expressed in MWh) supplied by S to premises in Great Britain'. From delivery year 2024/25, apportioned by share of Total Relevant Demand in the peak period (previously Total Gross Demand) | definition; charge in £/kW of peak-period demand share | Great Britain, statutory | The Electricity Capacity (Supplier Payment etc.) Regulations 2014, SI 2014/3354, reg. 2(1), https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/3354/regulation/2; LCCC Data Portal, Winter Peak Demand dataset, https://dp.lowcarboncontracts.uk/dataset/winter-peak-demand | **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 (Olly): a supplier-published pass-through rate HAS now been located** — see the row immediately below, 'Capacity Market supplier charge — published supplier pass-through rate (p/kWh on peak-window consumption)'. The retired note read 'No authoritative public £/kW supplier charge rate was locatable', and that absence is why every campaign site's `tariff_stack.levies` carried no Capacity Market line and priced the avoided cost at £0 — a D45 defect, not a conservatism. The £/kW framing was itself the obstacle: suppliers do not publish the obligation as £/kW of demand, they publish it as p/kWh **on peak-window consumption**, which is directly usable and needs no approximation. For scale on the auction side: T-1 for 2025/26 cleared at £20/kW/year for 7.9 GW de-rated; T-4 for 2028/29 at £60/kW/year for 43.1 GW de-rated (DESNZ, CfD and CM scheme update 2025). |
| Capacity Market supplier charge — published supplier pass-through rate (p/kWh on peak-window consumption) | 'Capacity Mechanism Peak Rate', initial billing rate, by delivery month: 30.581 p/kWh (April–May 2026); 31.382 p/kWh (June–July 2026); 18.042 p/kWh (February–March 2026); 18.064 p/kWh (October 2025–January 2026); 15.833–17.057 p/kWh (April–September 2025); 12.237–12.254 p/kWh (September 2024–March 2025); 5.313 p/kWh (March 2022, series low). Separate 'Operational Rate' 0.003 p/kWh throughout. | p/kWh, charged ONLY on 16:00–19:00 consumption on working days in November, December, January and February | Great Britain, half-hourly and non-half-hourly C&I supply. Verbatim: "Obligation Costs reflect the costs of the capacity auctions and are charged on consumption during the winter peak period; from 16:00 and 19:00 on working days between November & February." Charged on an Estimated Annual Consumption Volume across the peak period, then reconciled on a pass-through contract. | npower Business Solutions, 'Third Party and Industry Charges', Capacity Mechanism section and CM rates table, https://npowerbusinesssolutions.com/third-party-and-industry-charges (retrieved 2026-08-17); window corroborated by SI 2014/3354 reg. 2 (row above) | **The 5.9× spread is a TIME SERIES ACROSS CHARGING YEARS, not uncertainty about one year.** The rate steps every April and tracks the auction clearing price for the delivery year: 18.042 → 30.581 p/kWh at April 2026 is the record £63.00/kW/yr T-4 result for delivery year 2026/27. **Match the rate to the charging year the rest of the tariff stack is on** — a 2022 rate on a 2026/27 stack is the error, not a conservatism. CORROBORATION from this register's own auction rows: 43,000.955 MW × £63.00/kW + 7,192.317 MW × £5.00/kW = £2,745m unindexed, which at 30.581 p/kWh implies 8.98 TWh of Chargeable Demand = 35.2 GW averaged across the ~246–255 h window — plausible GB winter-peak demand net of the EII exclusion. TWO CAVEATS FOR UNDERWRITING: (1) the rate for the months actually charged (Nov 2026–Feb 2027) is NOT yet published — the table ends at July 2026 and the 2026/27 reconciliation reads 'TBC Jun-27'; historic reconciliations have moved an initial rate by up to ~10% both ways. (2) npower states HH CM cost is "either fully passed through (with subsequent reconciliation) or fixed into unit charges" — **on a fixed-price contract, in-window discharge does not reduce this year's bill**, so the avoided cost is only cash-real on pass-through terms. This is one supplier's published pass-through, not a universal rate; confirm against the site's own invoice. Consumed by `TariffStack.windowed_levies` (a levy charged inside a stated window only — it must NEVER go in the flat `levies` map, which the objective applies to every half-hour). |
| Modo Energy benchmark: value of DUoS optimisation for a battery, 2026/27 | Net positive in 4 of 14 distribution network regions at high voltage; net position ranges from +£9.8k/MW/year (South Eastern) to a cost of over £58k/MW/year (North of Scotland) | £ per MW per year | Great Britain, 2026/27 charging year, battery connected at high voltage, optimising around DUoS charges | Modo Energy, DUoS charges 2026/27: what is the value for battery energy storage?, 2026, https://modoenergy.com/research/en/gb-great-britain-duos-charges-battery-energy-storage-value-2026-27-distribution-use-charge | Figures from the public summary of a partially paywalled article. Applies to a battery on its own HV connection, not strictly a BTM asset behind a C&I meter, but it is the closest published benchmark and it says DUoS optimisation destroys value in most GB regions. |
| DUoS exceeded capacity formula (DCP161 implementation) | Demand exceeded capacity = max(2 × sqrt(AI² + max(RI,RE)²) − MIC, 0), where AI = active import (kWh), RI = reactive import (kVArh), RE = reactive export (kVArh), MIC = maximum import capacity (kVA). Calculated for every half hour; the maximum over the billing period is charged for the full duration of that billing period | kVA | NGED licence areas; DCP161 implemented across GB DNOs on 1 April 2018 | National Grid Electricity Distribution (East Midlands) plc, Use of System Charging Statement effective 1 April 2026, paras 2.41–2.43, https://commercial.nationalgrid.co.uk/downloads-view-reciteme/684677 | Only reactive values coincident with active import are used. Where multiple MPANs are aggregated for billing, HH consumption is summed before the calculation. |
| DUoS chargeable reactive power formula | Demand chargeable kVArh = max( max(RI,RE) − ( sqrt(1/0.95² − 1) × AI ), 0 ), i.e. threshold coefficient 0.328684 (33% of active energy, equivalent to power factor 0.95). Calculated every half hour and summed over the billing period | kVArh | NGED, LV and HV Designated Properties, HH metered MPANs | National Grid Electricity Distribution (East Midlands) plc, Use of System Charging Statement effective 1 April 2026, paras 2.48–2.53, https://commercial.nationalgrid.co.uk/downloads-view-reciteme/684677 | Square-root calculation specified to two decimal places. Only reactive values coincident with active import are used. |
| MIC reduction constraints | No reduction in MIC/MEC allowed for a 12-month period following agreement; reductions permitted only once in any 12-month period; applied from the start of the next billing period after the request is received | months | NGED licence areas | National Grid Electricity Distribution (East Midlands) plc, Use of System Charging Statement effective 1 April 2026, paras 2.38–2.40, https://commercial.nationalgrid.co.uk/downloads-view-reciteme/684677 | NGED warns that once reduced, 'the original capacity may not be available in the future without the need for network reinforcement and associated charges'. MIC reduction is therefore an irreversible option, not a free saving. |
| DCUSA Non-Final Demand Site qualifying criteria | 'The property is a Single Site at which either or both Electricity Storage and/or Electricity Generation occurs ... [and] has an export MPAN and an import MPAN with associated metering equipment which only measures export from Electricity Storage and/or Electricity Generation and import for or directly relating to Electricity Storage and/or Electricity Generation (and not export from another source and/or import for another activity)' | qualifying criteria (text) | DCUSA, applied by all GB DNOs; certification required from the Supplier Party (MPAS-registered) or the Customer/CVA Registrant (CMRS-registered) | National Grid Electricity Distribution (East Midlands) plc, Use of System Charging Statement effective 1 April 2026, Appendix 3 — Non-Final Demand Site Certificate of Compliance, and section 10, https://commercial.nationalgrid.co.uk/downloads-view-reciteme/684677 | Decisive for BTM: a commercial or industrial site with process or building load cannot satisfy 'not ... import for another activity', so it cannot obtain the 'No Residual' DUoS tariff. Qualification takes effect only from the date the DNO receives valid certification. |
