## Cost inputs, rating liability, and the private-wire residual — sub-MW GB C&I BTM storage (added 2026-08-17, Olly, the gb_v1_11 research pass)

**Why this section exists.** Every priced candidate on the eight campaign sites had a non-positive P50
cashflow in every year, and 54.5% of the fixed cost stack at 100 kW was unsourced placeholder
(`metering_idno` £1,200/site/yr + `dno_costs` £1,500/site/yr, both charged with **no size
multiplier**, so a 2 kW battery paid the same as a 750 kW one). This section records what the primary
sources actually say about those lines, and — because the same pass found the library's capex is
**below** every published GB benchmark — the capex evidence that cancels the saving. Both landed
together in `assumptions/gb_v1_11.yaml` (decision D49). **Reading one half without the other
manufactures a positive return out of half a correction.**

| Constant | Value | Units | Basis / conditions | Source | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Metering + data cost of an EXISTING GB half-hourly supply | **~£400 per meter per annum in aggregate** (MOP + DC + DA combined) | £/meter/yr | GB non-domestic half-hourly metered supplies, guidance page last updated 29/08/2025. Stated of supplies that are ALREADY HH metered, in the context of MHHS role changes. | LASER Energy (the public-sector energy buying framework operated by Commercial Services Kent Ltd, wholly owned by Kent County Council), 'Market-wide Half Hourly Settlement', https://www.laserenergy.org.uk/market-wide-half-hourly-settlement/ | Verbatim: 'which are ~£400 per meter per annum in aggregate'. Re-verified by direct fetch 2026-08-17 (Olly), independently of the research pass. A buying framework's published figure, not a supplier tariff sheet — no primary MOP/DC/DA schedule could be sourced (see NOT FOUND below). It is a **HOST-SITE** cost: incremental to a no-export BTM battery it is £0, because the battery adds no boundary Metering System. |
| MHHS role change (why 'MOP/DC/DA' is stale terminology) | Supplier agent functions move from meter operator / data collector / data aggregator to metering and data service provider; settlement aggregation moves inside central systems | n/a (rule) | GB, market-wide half-hourly settlement programme. | Ofgem, 'Market-wide Half-Hourly Settlement: Final Impact Assessment', 20 April 2021, ¶3.35, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/docs/2021/04/mhss_final_impact_assessment_final_version_for_publication_20.04.21_1_0.pdf | The same IA contains **no per-MPAN metering-agent £ figure**; its only per-MPAN cost remark ('an annual fee in the region of 10s of pounds per MPAN') is for NHH **unmetered** data aggregation, a different service. |
| DNO recurring per-site operating charge for a connection | **Does not exist as a recurring charge.** Verbatim: 'The cost of future operations, repairs and maintenance of the connection assets and our Distribution System is normally recovered through the on-going charges for use of system you pay as part of your quarterly or monthly electricity supply bill' (clause 6.4). Above-Minimum-Scheme O&M 'would normally be levied as a **one-off charge** representing the net present value of the future operation and maintenance costs' (clause 5.12) | n/a (charging rule) | NGED South West licence area, charging statement effective June 2026. Worked examples add the O&M percentage ONCE to the Connection Charge (e.g. 'Operations & maintenance @ 20%* of £12,300 = £2,460'), the 20% being explicitly illustrative. | National Grid Electricity Distribution (South West) plc, 'Statement of Methodology and Charges for Connection…', June 2026, clauses 5.12, 6.4, 6.5, https://connections.nationalgrid.co.uk/downloads/229320/south-west-june-26-statement-of-methodology-and-charges.pdf | **Decisive for modelling: a flat annual per-site DNO line is either a double count of DUoS (which is priced separately) or a one-off entered in an annual field.** The genuine recurring DNO cost a battery can cause is a higher DUoS **capacity** charge if it raises MIC/MEC — that belongs in the DUoS lines. |
| DNO one-off Assessment & Design charges (minimum), NGED South West | Single Small Scale Embedded Generator £193; other generation ≤20 kVA LV £410; generation >20–50 kVA LV £1,142; **'Connection of other generation greater than 50kVA at LV' £1,557** (row verbatim); generation ≤250 kVA HV £1,960; >250 kVA–1 MVA HV £1,968. Demand comparators: LV >100–250 kVA £665; LV >250 kVA–1 MVA £772 | £ per application (one-off) | NGED South West, Section 7 Table D, effective June 2026. A battery applies as **generation**. These are MINIMUM charges, not quotes. | Same document as the row above, Section 7 Table D | The whole campaign sits in the '>50 kVA at LV generation' band, so **£1,557** is the applicable published minimum — within 4% of the £1,500/**yr** the library carried, which is the signature of a one-off in an annual field (an observation, not a provenance claim). Witness testing, commissioning and any capitalised O&M percentage are additional. Other DNO areas publish their own tables. |
| DNO treatment of operations/repairs/maintenance, second DNO | 'Operations, repairs and maintenance' is Non-Contestable Work the DNO performs; scheme-specific ongoing costs (e.g. communications) are marked 'We fund' for all three flexible-connection types; recurring O&M charges appear only for **Speculative Developments** (¶5.49) | n/a (charging rule) | Northern Powergrid (Northeast) licence area, February 2024. | Northern Powergrid (Northeast) plc, Connection Charging Methodology Statement, February 2024, row 28 and ¶5.49, https://www.northernpowergrid.com/sites/default/files/assets/Northern%20Powergrid%20(Northeast)%20Connection%20Charging%20Methodology%20Statement%20February%202024.pdf | Independent corroboration of the NGED position from a second DNO. 'Speculative Developments' is a housing-development category, not a battery at an existing commercial site. |
| GB C&I behind-the-meter battery capex | **£350/kWh** installed, with an LCoS of **about 16p/kWh** on the same system | £/kWh; p/kWh | Great Britain, C&I behind-the-meter, dated 28 January 2025. Worked example is 'a small supermarket in Maidstone, Kent that consumes 470MWh per year'. At a 2 h duration this is **£700/kW = £700,000/MW**. | Gridcog, 'The economics of behind-the-meter battery storage. Part 1: Reducing your energy bill', 28 January 2025, https://www.gridcog.com/blog/the-economics-of-behind-the-meter-battery-storage-part-1-reducing-your-energy-bill | **A software vendor's blog, not a survey or primary document, and it is the ONLY attributable GB C&I capex figure found** — a second independent source was searched for and NOT FOUND, so anything resting on it is single-sourced and belongs at `needs_review`/low confidence. It is internally consistent (£350/kWh against 16p/kWh LCoS) and already cited elsewhere in this register. Its £700/kW is **13% above** Modo's grid-scale 2 h benchmark (£618/kW, 2025), which is the right direction: fixed costs (connection, controls, commissioning) do not scale down, so sub-MW BTM costs MORE per kW than grid scale. |
| Storage cost composition, DESNZ-commissioned taxonomy | Capex ~**70%** of LCOS, O&M ~**30%**. Fixed O&M defined as 'yearly minor maintenance including inspections, filter replacements, alignments, and in some cases software upgrade and replacement of components'. **Metering sits in CAPEX**: 'Grid integration, including costs associated with connecting the ESS to the grid, including transformer cost, metering, and isolation breakers' | % of LCOS; scope definitions | UK, commissioned by DESNZ, delivered by a consortium led by the Carbon Trust with Mott MacDonald, UCL and Pengwern Associates, June 2025, p.31. | DESNZ, 'Energy Innovation Needs Assessment: Energy Storage' (RAF134/2223), June 2025, https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/69449d0c9273c48f554cf642/einas-2025-energy-storage.pdf | Gives composition, **no £ figures** for GB sub-MW. Two modelling consequences: the UK government's own storage taxonomy has **no annual metering line and no annual DNO line at all**, and its fixed-O&M scope matches a narrow O&M key (excluding insurance, rates, metering, network). Notable negative from the same pass: **DESNZ 'Electricity Generation Costs 2025' (published January 2026) does not cover electricity storage at all** — every 'storage' mention is CCS or hydrogen. |
| Business rates: when battery plant and machinery is EXEMPT | 'Excepted renewables plant and machinery' = plant used for 'the generation, **storage**, transformation or transmission of power **where the sources of energy or technologies relied upon are mainly or exclusively** — (i) biomass; (ii) biofuels; (iii) biogas; (iv) fuel cells; (v) photovoltaics; (vi) water (including waves and tides); (vii) wind; (viii) solar power; (ix) geothermal; (x) heat from air, water or the ground'; plus 'excepted EVCP plant and machinery'. Exemption applies 'in relation to any day before **1st April 2035**' | n/a (statutory test) | England (the instrument amends the England 2000 Regulations; Wales differs — Welsh Government material proposes exemption from 1 April 2024). In force 1 April 2022, made under s.143(2) and Sch.6 ¶2(8) Local Government Finance Act 1988. | The Valuation for Rating (Plant and Machinery) (England) (Amendment) Regulations 2022, **SI 2022/405**, reg. 2(b), https://legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2022/405/pdfs/uksi_20220405_en.pdf; Explanatory Memorandum ¶¶2.1, 7.2, 7.4, https://legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2022/405/pdfs/uksiem_20220405_en.pdf | **Decisive three-way answer.** Charged mainly/exclusively from on-site renewables → EXEMPT to 31 Mar 2035, rates line £0. Mainly/exclusively for an EVCP → EXEMPT. **Charged from the public grid → RATEABLE**, because the grid mix is not 'mainly or exclusively' any listed source; that is the priced-floor case. Whether **landfill gas** is 'biogas' for limb (iii) is not defined in the instrument and is a rating-surveyor question — live for Knowl Hill (recorded at 92.1% landfill-gas supply) and for the two private-wire EfW sites. Treat as a per-site DATA ASK; never model the exemption without the surveyor's answer. |
| Small Business Rate Relief eligibility (why it does not help a multi-site host) | 100% relief below £12,000 RV, tapering to 0% between £12,001 and £15,000, and only 'if that's the only property your business uses'. Where a business uses more than one property, relief continues only if 'none of your other properties have a rateable value above **£2,899**' **and** 'the total rateable value of all your properties is less than **£20,000** (£28,000 in London)'. 12 months' continuation after acquiring a second property (36 months if acquired on/after 27 November 2025) | £ RV thresholds | England, current guidance (no publication date shown on the page). | gov.uk, 'Apply for business rate relief — Small business rate relief', https://www.gov.uk/apply-for-business-rate-relief/small-business-rate-relief | **SBRR is NOT a live mitigant for these hosts and should stop being treated as one:** a multi-site waste operator or a science campus fails the £2,899 and £20,000 tests on its existing premises long before the battery is considered — and if the battery is not a separate hereditament, SBRR is irrelevant to it in any case. |
| Capacity Market supplier charge — the published PASS-THROUGH rate and its window | Rate name 'Capacity Mechanism Peak Rate (p/kWh)', values ranging **5.313–30.581 p/kWh** across delivery months January 2021 to July 2026, applied to consumption 'during the winter peak period; from 16:00 and 19:00 on working days between November & February' | p/kWh on in-window import | GB, one licensed supplier's published third-party charges schedule. Footnotes distinguish 'Initial billing rate', 'Reconciliation based on actual volumes' and 'Potential reconciliation reserved for extreme industry event'. | npower Business Solutions, 'Third Party and Industry Charges', https://npowerbusinesssolutions.com/third-party-and-industry-charges (WebFetched twice with independent prompts, 2026-08-17; no publication date on the page) | **Range endpoints confirmed by both fetches; individual monthly cells were read via an extraction model and must be re-read from the live table before any figure is entered anywhere.** Consistent with the statutory base: SI 2014/3354 reg. 2 defines 'period of high demand' as '4 p.m. to 7 p.m. on any working day in November, December, January or February', and Chargeable Demand as Active Import 'with no adjustment made for any Active Export'. Order-of-magnitude sanity check: ~£1.5–2bn/yr recovered from ~8–10 TWh of in-window volume ⇒ ~20 p/kWh, so 17–30 p/kWh is the right magnitude, not a typo. **Supplier-specific**: another supplier may recover CM flat across all consumption, in which case a peak-shaving battery avoids far less. Per-site contract check required; the ask is precisely 'does the CM line appear as a peak-period p/kWh rate?' **STATE OF THE REPO, 2026-08-17:** the seven grid-supplied site records now carry `tariff_stack.windowed_levies.CM_peak` = **0.30581 GBP/kWh** with months 11/12/1/2 and a 16:00-19:00 weekday window, landed by the concurrent windowed-levy engine work. **That is the MAXIMUM of the published range and one of the extraction-read monthly cells (April 2026), i.e. the most revenue-flattering figure available in the source.** The April 2025 cell read 17.057 p/kWh and the range floor is 5.313 p/kWh, so the choice of cell spans a factor of 5.8 on the avoided-cost value. Re-read the live table by eye, use a RECONCILED rather than an initial billing rate, and record which - the direction of the error runs UP on revenue. |
| Levy values on the campaign site records: which reconcile and which do not | **CCL 0.00801 £/kWh — CORRECT** (exactly HMRC's 2026/27 main rate). **AAHEDC 0.00044201 — CORRECT** (exactly NESO's 2026/27 forecast tariff). **RO 0.03273 — CORRECT** (matches both this register's DESNZ-derived 0.472 × £69.34 and npower's published 3.273 p/kWh for Apr–Jun 2026, to 5 s.f.). **BSUoS 0.013115 — MATCHES NO PUBLISHED TARIFF PERIOD** (Tariff 6, Oct 25–Mar 26, is £15.69/MWh; Tariff 7, Apr–Sep 26, is £13.74/MWh; £13.115 is neither — reconcile to a named six-month tariff or record the blend). **CfD_ILR 0.01 — ROUND-NUMBER SHAPED**, treat as a placeholder and source it to a named LCCC quarter. **NuclearRAB 0.004683 — NO SOURCE FOUND** (this register's only Nuclear RAB figure is the *operational costs* levy at £0.0063/MWh, three orders of magnitude smaller and a different charge). **FiT_UNCONFIRMED 0.0049** — self-labelled unconfirmed; npower publishes 0.831 p/kWh for April 2026. **Capacity Market — was ABSENT ENTIRELY** (so it priced at £0); as at 2026-08-17 the seven grid-supplied records carry it in the separate `windowed_levies` map at 0.30581 £/kWh, not in the flat `levies` sum. **FiT is inconsistent across the records**: `harwell.json` carries `FiT: 0.008` while the other six carry `FiT_UNCONFIRMED: 0.0049`, a 0.31 p/kWh spread on the same national levy | £/kWh | GB grid-supplied campaign sites, `examples/real/sites/*.json` → `tariff_stack.levies`, as at 2026-08-17. | HMRC/GOV.UK 'Climate Change Levy rates'; NESO 2026/27 AAHEDC forecast tariff; DESNZ 'Calculating the level of the RO for 2026 to 2027'; Drax/NESO BSUoS fixed tariffs; npower Business Solutions schedule (above) — all as carried elsewhere in this register | **Data ask, not a library change: these values live in the site records, not in `assumptions/`.** RO alone is 44% of the 7.388 p/kWh stack, and the three flagged lines are 27% of it. The exact RO match to a supplier schedule suggests the stack was transcribed from one, which is why the missing CM line reads as an omission rather than a reasoned exclusion. Note the CfD error (+0.29 p) and FiT error (−0.34 p) very nearly cancel, so the flat stack is roughly right in total; the material defect is the missing **time-windowed** levy. |

### NOT FOUND — searches run 2026-08-17 and recorded so they are not repeated

| What could not be found | Best available substitute, and what was NOT done |
|---|---|
| A **primary** (Ofgem / Elexon / supplier tariff-sheet) published annual MOP + DC + DA charge for a GB HH site | LASER Energy's ~£400/meter/yr (above). Six search formulations plus a full-text pass of Ofgem's MHHS Final IA for `per MPAN` / `metering agent` / `MOP` / `data collect`. |
| Explicit Elexon/BSC confirmation that a no-export BTM battery needs **no new MPAN** | Inferred from P375's existence and design, and stated as an inference rather than quoted as a rule. `bscdocs.elexon.co.uk` serves no body text to automated fetch. |
| A published **£/MW or £/kW rateable value** scheme for BESS | None. Confirms the existing register note. VOA publishes SCat 733 and the contractor's-basis method but no rate; four search reformulations plus two VOA Rating Manual fetches. `business_rates.annual_gbp_per_mw` therefore stays a placeholder and **no replacement number was invented**. |
| Whether a BTM battery is a **separate hereditament** or merges into the host's assessment | Unresolved in published guidance; a rating-surveyor question. Material: if it merges, the £/MW may be a **host** cost the project never sees. |
| A **GB BESS insurance rate-on-line** from an insurer or broker market report | None better than the existing Solarif 0.5–3% (LOW AUTHORITY). Four reformulations across GCube, NARDAC, Marsh, Aon, Brown & Brown. The only rate-like figure found was **US solar** (0.10–0.30% of insured value) and it was **not transferred**. |
| A published **small-scale O&M premium** for sub-MW BTM (£/kW/yr or £/MW/yr) | None. DESNZ EGC 2025 does not cover storage; EINAS 2025 gives composition only. Direction is up, magnitude unsourced, so `battery_opex.annual_fixed_opex` was **left unchanged rather than tuned**. |
| A **second independent GB C&I capex source** to corroborate Gridcog's £350/kWh | Only Reonic (installer guide, £200–450/kWh commercial, snippet). **So the +40% capex correction in gb_v1_11 is single-sourced.** One real installer quote for a 100–300 kW GB commercial system settles the largest open number in the model. |
| **Lease / asset-finance terms** for sub-MW commercial BESS | None. Every observed GB BESS facility in this register is ≥£42.7m against £17.5k–£525k assets, and CEPA received no investor responses on lithium-BESS return on debt. **The absence is the finding: these sites underwrite unlevered.** |
| £/kW/yr per stream for sub-MW BTM (DUoS avoidance, ToU arbitrage, MIC/ASC avoidance, DFS, DNO flex) | Does not exist at this scale. The Gridcog DNO-flex row (£1.90–9.20/kW/yr on a 250 kW battery) is the only sub-MW £/yr row here; everything else published is grid-scale whole-stack and **not transferable**. |

Documents that block automated retrieval (recorded so a future session does not waste the call):
`bscdocs.elexon.co.uk` (metadata only), `emrsettlement.co.uk` (HTTP 403), `ember-energy.org` PDFs (403).
**One misattribution caught and discarded:** a search snippet attributed the '~£400 per meter per
annum' figure to a Milton Keynes Council / LASER framework pack; the PDF was fetched and
text-extracted and **the figure is not in it**. The phrase traces to LASER Energy's own MHHS page,
which was then fetched and quoted directly.

### OPEN QUESTION, NOT A CONSTANT — the private-wire non-commodity residual is priced as a marginal p/kWh, and GB regulated residuals are not

Two campaign sites carry a single `levies` entry that is **not a levy**:
`tanhouse.json` → `private_wire_non_commodity_residual` = **13.13958 p/kWh**, and
`colnbrook.json` → **4.13135 p/kWh**. Because `finance.cashflows._levy_rate_gbp_per_kwh` and its
mirror in `optimisation.milp_solver` sum `levies.values()` into one flat £/kWh that the MILP
objective applies to **every** half-hour, that figure prices a **marginal kWh of charging**. Both
sites' own records say the composition is unknown: each is 'a DERIVED RESIDUAL, not a sourced rate…
the arithmetic difference between two invoice figures', back-solved from a blended invoiced delivered
cost minus a volume-weighted commodity cost.

**What the GB literature says about the word 'residual', and it points one way.** Since the Targeted
Charging Review, GB network **residual** charges are **fixed £/site/day banded by capacity — not
per-kWh**. This register already holds that: the TDR residual band tables by LV/HV MIC in kVA, and
'Last Triad period: February 2023. Fixed £/site/day TDR effective from April 2023'. Ofgem made
residuals fixed precisely so they would stop distorting consumption decisions. **A fixed £/day charge
has zero marginal cost per kWh: adding a kWh of battery charging does not increase it at all.**

**Why that is not yet a finding.** Both supplies are **private wire** (EfW, plus solar at Tanhouse),
where the charge components are **contractual rather than regulated**, so nothing in the GB
regulatory literature establishes that this specific charge is fixed. The grid-supplied sites in the
same portfolio do handle this correctly: their genuinely fixed elements sit in
`standing_charge_per_day_gbp` and `capacity_charge_gbp_per_kva_day`, **outside** the per-kWh levy sum.

**Why it matters more than anything else in this section.** Tanhouse's recorded diagnosis is that
round-trip losses are bought at the full delivered 19.49 p/kWh (commodity plus the 13.14 p flat
residual), which raises the spread a cycle must clear to **4.788 p/kWh** against the **3.525 p/kWh**
its own commodity rates offer — so 'the optimiser declines to cycle at any size'. Colnbrook offers
3.086 p/kWh on the same test. **If any part of that residual is a standing charge, a capacity charge
or a fixed £/day residual, that part must not price a marginal kWh, the break-even falls, and both
sites' verdicts reopen.** For scale: Tanhouse's 13.14 p/kWh residual is 3.2× Colnbrook's, on the same
private wire, with commodity rates differing by only 0.5 p/kWh — a gap the records call unexplained
and material.

**The test is cheap and decisive, and it is a DATA ASK, not a derivation:** obtain one full
private-wire invoice for each site with every line item, and the supply contract. Then split the
residual into (a) genuinely volumetric components, which stay in `levies`, and (b) fixed £/day or
£/kVA/day components, which move to `standing_charge_per_day_gbp` /
`capacity_charge_gbp_per_kva_day` where they cannot price a marginal kWh. **Not actioned here, and
deliberately so:** re-shaping the charge requires the dispatch objective to be changed (a file another
lane holds) and it moves money in the FAVOURABLE direction on two sites whose 'no viable
configuration' verdicts have been published, so it needs the owner's decision, not a unilateral edit.
Direction of effect if the split turns out to be partly fixed: **charging cost DOWN, break-even spread
DOWN, both sites' modelled revenue UP.**

## Avoided agreed capacity and the TCR band step — the two capacity-priced legs a BTM battery CAN reach (added 2026-08-17, Olly, MICASC-3)

Every number in this section is either published-and-pinned or **measured on the sites' own metered
years** by the agreed-capacity overlay solve (`MilpDispatchSolver`). None is estimated.

**What was wrong before.** Both solvers computed the agreed-capacity charge as
`capacity_charge × (asc_kw or mic_kw) × 365.25` — a battery-invariant constant — so peak shaving
could never reduce it, and `MIC_ASC_HEADROOM` returned £0 unconditionally on the reasoning that
headroom value is realised through connection-bounded arbitrage. That reasoning covers the
**arbitrage** reading of headroom and only it. Two capacity-priced legs were unmodelled.

**The counterfactual is the whole discipline of this number.** A site billed on an agreed capacity
far above its metered peak can release that capacity **today, with no battery**, by asking the DNO.
That is the far bigger figure on every real site, and it is **not battery revenue**. Only the
increment below it is.

| Constant | Value | Units | Basis / conditions | Source | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Agreed-capacity charge, the two live licence areas | NGED West Midlands LV Site Specific 10.66; SSEN SEPD LV Site Specific 10.63 | p/kVA/day | GB 2026/27, LV Site Specific HH. Equals **£38.94** and **£38.83 per kVA per year** — the per-kVA value of any peak reduction the customer can convert into a lower agreed capacity. | `assumptions/gb_v1_11.yaml duos.unit_rates_p_per_kwh_by_dno.<AREA>.capacity_charge_p_per_kva_day`, per-area 2026/27 Use of System Charging Statement with URL + sha256 | Every real site's own evidenced `tariff_stack.capacity_charge_gbp_per_kva_day` (0.1066 / 0.1063) reproduces the published LV rate **exactly**, which is what evidences the LV table for sites whose `voltage_level` is null. Exceeded-capacity charges equal the capacity charge in every area fetched, so a breach of a reduced capacity costs the saving back at the same rate. |
| TCR band step, total of BOTH banded lines, NGED West Midlands LV | band_4→band_3 **£8,692/yr**; band_3→band_2 **£1,048/yr**; band_2→band_1 **£2,417/yr** | GBP/year | GB 2026/27. Sum of the TNUoS non-locational demand residual band and the DUoS banded fixed line, both allocated on the same agreed capacity. | `tnuos.demand_residual_bands_gbp_per_day` (NESO Jan-2026 Final TNUoS 2026/27 Table 10) + `duos...NGED_WM.fixed_p_per_mpan_day` (NGED WM 2026/27 statement) | **The MICASC audit costed this step at the DUoS line ALONE — £329/yr for band_2→band_1 — and missed the TNUoS residual (£2,087/yr on the same step). The audit understates the band leg 7.3×.** Both lines move together because both are banded on agreed capacity. |
| Standing-charge reconstruction, proving the band lines are charges these sites actually pay | bc_mrf 38.180103 (TNUoS LV4) + 5.8321 (DUoS WM band_4) = **44.012203** = the site's recorded standing charge, exactly. bc_ash 11.511840 + 2.1977 = **13.70954**, exactly. ewelme 14.381374 = TNUoS LV3 exactly (SEPD publishes 0.00 DUoS fixed at every LV band). | GBP/day | Six of nine installed real sites reconstruct exactly (tolerance 5e-4). | This repo, `examples/real/sites/*.json` against `assumptions/gb_v1_11.yaml` | This is the check that licenses claiming a saving on these lines at all: a band step is only worth £ if the site is actually paying the band. **Two sites carry no standing charge (colnbrook, tanhouse) and one MISMATCHES: harwell carries LV4 38.180103 against a 213.81 kVA MIC, which the published thresholds put in band 3 (LV3 = 14.381374). Harwell's standing charge is overstated ~£8,690/yr — a site-data defect, logged, not fixed here.** |
| Security margin between a reduced agreed capacity and the modelled worst-case net import | 10% of rated battery power | fraction | **A LABELLED MODELLING CONVENTION, NOT A PUBLISHED RATE.** No GB source specifies one and none was invented. Covers the battery being unavailable or empty at the moment the site peaks. | This repo, `gb_streams._CAPACITY_SECURITY_MARGIN_FRACTION` | Direction of effect **DOWN** — it reduces the claim. Expressed against rated power because the risk scales with how much of the peak the battery is carrying. Needs the owner's confirmation. |
| Peak reduction the EXISTING floor dispatch delivers, measured | 7.4 kW system: **0.000 kW**; 25 kW: **0.000 kW**; 50 kW: 0.76 kW; 100 kW: 32.06 kW (Bishops Cleeve MRF) | kW | The `_EPS_PEAK` tie-break minimises the residual peak only among schedules already tied on revenue. | This repo, measured 2026-08-17 | **Decisive: pricing this leg off the FLOOR schedule would report £0 at every size the sizing grid explores.** Shifting discharge onto the annual peak costs real red-band money and an epsilon cannot pay for it. An overlay solve with the capacity rate in the objective is required, not optional. |
| Battery-attributable value, measured on the overlay (net of sacrificed firm revenue) | bc_mrf 7.4 kW **£272/yr**, 25 kW £891, 100 kW £1,557. knowl_hill_invoice_anchored 10 kW £349, 25 kW **£2,936** (incl. £2,087 band step), 50 kW £3,229. harwell 10 kW £342, 25 kW £803. tanhouse 10 kW £349, 50 kW £1,747. | GBP/year | 2 h duration, gb_v1_11, each site's own metered year. Incremental to the no-battery renegotiation; firm revenue the overlay sacrifices is subtracted. | This repo, measured 2026-08-17 | **CONDITIONAL — outside the priced floor, so it moves no IRR, DSCR or candidate selection.** At bc_mrf 7.4 kW this is +53% on a £518/yr firm floor; at knowl_hill_invoice_anchored 25 kW it is **15× the site's entire firm floor** (£191/yr). Harwell has **0.00 kW of MIC headroom** and still earns here — the discriminator for this leg is a shaveable PEAK, not headroom. |
| Renegotiation-only saving available with NO battery, measured | bc_mrf **£15,039/yr**; knowl_hill_invoice_anchored **£18,632/yr**; ewelme £8,183; knowl_hill £12,459; ewelme_invoice_anchored £13,395; tanhouse £505; harwell £0 | GBP/year | Moving the agreed capacity from what the site is billed on today to the tightest whole kVA its own metered peak already supports, band step included. | This repo, measured 2026-08-17 | **NOT BATTERY MONEY, and crediting it to the battery is the single largest overstatement available on this stream** — it is 55× the battery's own contribution at bc_mrf 7.4 kW. Reported so it can never be double-sold, and excluded from the priced figure. It is, separately, a large and immediate customer saving worth raising commercially. |
| **Measured effect on a REAL `gridq run`, the number that matters for underwriting** | Bishops Cleeve MRF, gb_v1_11, before vs after: best project IRR **−4.1268% → −4.1268%**; best annual margin **£429.34 → £429.34**; positive-margin candidates **57 → 57**; selected candidate **none → none**. **Byte-identical.** The new stream is live on **30 of 31** confirmed candidates (£272–£4,004/yr) and correctly £0 on all 150 typical-period proxy points. Knowl Hill (invoice-anchored): live on **27 of 27** confirmed, £21–£3,961/yr, against a priced floor of £4,106/yr at the best point; no IRR resolves on that site at any size, before or after. | GBP/year, % | `python -m gridq.cli run examples/real/sites/<site>.json --assumptions gb_v1_11` | This repo, measured 2026-08-17/18 | **This invariance is the design working, not the change failing.** CONDITIONAL revenue is outside `finance.cashflows._PRICED_FIRMNESS`, so it cannot move P50, P90, the DSCR, the IRR or candidate selection — and it must not, because nobody has applied to the DNO. The lane's output is **gated upside plus a commercial finding**, not a better IRR. At the best Bishops Cleeve MRF candidate the conditional stream is £272/yr against a £481/yr priced floor (+57% on reported value, none of it bankable). |
| Overlay runtime cost | A full-series solve goes **3.98 s → 9.65 s (2.43x)** with the overlay | seconds | Bishops Cleeve MRF, 25 kW / 2 h, gb_v1_11. The overlay is a second full-year LP. | This repo, measured 2026-08-17 | Only full-series solves pay it: typical-period proxy solves refuse **before** solving (an annual worst-case peak is not observable on a weighted subset), so the sizing search is unaffected and only the confirm stage roughly doubles. |

**Per-site, all nine installed real sites, measured at a common 25 kW / 2 h probe on gb_v1_11 (2026-08-17/18).** Reported honestly, including where the answer is small:

| Site | Metered peak (kW) | Agreed (kVA) | Battery-attributable £/yr (NET) | of which TCR band | Site's whole FIRM floor £/yr | Renegotiation-only £/yr (NOT battery money) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bishops_cleeve_mrf | 147.48 | 250 | 891 | 0 | 1,751 | **15,039** |
| bishops_cleeve_ash_plant | 46.32 | 93 | 875 | 0 | 1,331 | 4,208 |
| ewelme | 69.28 | 200 | 468 | 0 | 1,517 | 8,183 |
| ewelme_invoice_anchored | 404.84 | 750 | 854 | 0 | 1,927 | 13,395 |
| harwell | 213.81 | 213.81 | 803 | 0 | 1,885 | 0 |
| knowl_hill | 227.20 | 325 | **893** | 0 | **294** | 12,459 |
| knowl_hill_invoice_anchored | 95.99 | 325 | **2,936** | **2,087** | **191** | **18,632** |
| tanhouse | 486.35 | 500 | 893 | 0 | **0** | 505 |
| colnbrook | 915.08 | 915.08 | 741 | 0 | **0** | 0 |

Four things this table says that a single headline would hide. **(1) Every site gets something**, because every site has a shaveable peak and the linear £/kVA leg needs no band crossing — the prior expectation that sites without MIC headroom would get nothing is **wrong for this leg**: Harwell has 0.00 kW of headroom (peak == MIC) and earns £803/yr, because the discriminator here is a shaveable PEAK, not headroom. **(2) Only one site reaches a TCR band step** at a plausible size: knowl_hill_invoice_anchored needs a ~16 kW shave to cross LV band 2 → 1; bishops_cleeve_mrf needs 68 kW, harwell 64, knowl_hill 78, ewelme_invoice_anchored 174, tanhouse 256, colnbrook 685; ewelme and bishops_cleeve_ash_plant are already in the lowest band, so £0 there is **permanent and not a data ask**. **(3) On three sites this conditional stream exceeds the entire FIRM floor** — knowl_hill 3.0×, knowl_hill_invoice_anchored 15.4×, and tanhouse and colnbrook have a firm floor of exactly £0 — which is worth knowing precisely because none of it is bankable. **(4) The renegotiation-only column dwarfs the battery column on six of nine sites** and is the commercially larger finding of the whole exercise.

## DFS 2026/27 ZONAL OUTTURN, measured per zone on the OBSERVED period count (added 2026-08-19, Olly)

**Why this section exists.** Every row in the section above is winter 2024/25, which PREDATES the
9 April 2026 zonal, bi-directional redesign, and the outstanding data ask two sections below asked for
exactly this: "a current cleared DFS utilisation-price distribution by zone". **That ask is now
CLOSED** by NESO's own utilisation report for the live 2026/27 season, held in this repo.

**Source, and the retrieval.** NESO Data Portal, package `demand-flexibility` ("Demand Flexibility
Service", NESO Open Data Licence), resource "DFS Utilisation Report" (live 2026/27 season),
resource_id `3ebf77d7-05df-466e-a023-dc45a90efeea`, portal
<https://www.neso.energy/data-portal/demand-flexibility-service-dfs>, bulk CSV
`https://api.neso.energy/datastore/dump/3ebf77d7-05df-466e-a023-dc45a90efeea`. Fetched 2026-08-18,
held at `data/fetched/neso/dfs_util_cur.csv`, sha256
`655bd61601692045a9e36e87534ccace13406fb7610d66f64d0a509a58edf280`. 7,369 bid rows, 70 Event IDs,
delivery 2026-04-14 to 2026-08-18 inclusive (127 days), 11 registered participants, 80 DFS Units, all
12 zones populated, 5,113 accepted / 2,256 rejected. Recomputed by
`scripts/derive_dfs_zonal_basis.py --basis`, which refuses to run if the sha256 has moved, and pinned
to the module constants by `tests/test_dfs_zonal_basis.py`.

**Filters, each of which changes the answer.** `Status == Accepted` (a rejected bid procured nothing);
`Service Requirement Type == Live` (a Test event is not a market outturn); `Event Type == Downwards`
(the dispatch measures DFS on NET DISCHARGE, which is the downwards direction; the file's 10 accepted
Upwards bids total 1.0 MW at GBP 20-45/MWh and are the other direction of a bi-directional product).
The direction filter is why zone 3 reads 163 settlement periods and not 173.

**Definitions, because two of them have a near neighbour that is wrong.** An *accepted settlement
period* is a DISTINCT `(Delivery Date, From_Local)` cell carrying an accepted bid in that zone, NOT a
bid-row count: zone 6's 948 accepted rows collapse to 407 distinct periods across 9 DFS Units, so rows
would overstate the call count about 2.3x. An *event day* is a distinct delivery date carrying one,
which is NESO's own definition of an "event". The price is VOLUME-WEIGHTED (cash over energy), which
is the figure that reconciles; DFS is PAY-AS-BID (Procurement Rules v5.0 para 12.2, and confirmed
empirically: 41.1% of accepted (event, zone, period) cells contain more than one distinct accepted
price), so an accepted bid IS what the provider receives.

**THE OBSERVED COUNT IS USED AS THE ANNUAL COUNT.** It is NOT annualised by 365/127. It is a MEASURED
FLOOR and is labelled as one: this many periods were procured in this zone in 127 days, so a
persisting year-round event rate would make it an understatement, and annualising would have
overstated it if the observed window was seasonally intense. The evidence does not settle which, and a
full delivery year of the 2026/27 service does not exist yet.

| DFS zone | accepted settlement periods (127 d) | event days | periods per event day | volume-weighted accepted price (GBP/MWh) | GBP/kW/yr at full delivery | postcode districts derived into this zone |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 82 | 14 | 5.86 | 209.699 | **8.60** | IV1 |
| 2 | 90 | 15 | 6.00 | 222.150 | **10.00** | AB10 |
| 3 | 163 | 25 | 6.52 | 210.495 | **17.16** | EH1, G1 |
| 4 | 206 | 32 | 6.44 | 196.525 | **20.24** | CA1, DH1, NE1 |
| 5 | 316 | 42 | 7.52 | 161.253 | **25.48** | LA1, LL30, M1 |
| 6 | 407 | 50 | 8.14 | 159.711 | **32.50** | B1, LE17, LS1, NG1, SK1 |
| 7 | 243 | 36 | 6.75 | 171.867 | **20.88** | HU1 |
| 8 | 282 | 44 | 6.41 | 185.889 | **26.21** | CF10, SA1 |
| 9 | 233 | 34 | 6.85 | 194.654 | **22.68** | PL1, TR1 |
| 10 | 370 | 51 | 7.25 | 163.979 | **30.34** | BS1, GL52, OX10, OX11, RG10, SO14 |
| 11 | 379 | 50 | 7.58 | 157.411 | **29.83** | NR1, PE1 |
| 12 | 366 | 47 | 7.79 | 162.533 | **29.74** | ME1, SL3, SW1A |

**Range GBP 8.60 (zone 1) to GBP 32.50 (zone 6) per kW/yr, median GBP 24.08.** The
INVERSE RELATIONSHIP is the point and it is what a national average destroys: the thin zones (1, 2, 3,
9) clear GBP 195-222/MWh on almost no volume, and the deep zones (6, 11, 12) clear GBP 157-164/MWh on
nearly all of it. A site's zone therefore sets both its price and its number of calls, in opposite
directions, so one zone's count on another zone's price manufactures a figure no zone could pay.

**Zone allocation is point-in-polygon, and the geography's hash reproduces.** NESO, 'DFS 12 Zones
GeoJson Map', <https://www.neso.energy/document/376656/download> - a zip containing
`12_Zones_DFS.geojson`, CRS84 (WGS84 lon/lat), 12 features each carrying the single property
`{'Region': 1..12}`. Fetched 2026-08-19; the zip's sha256 is
`ed3fa1cc89484faff47ff90fdb89c74d0fa860ff9818e729d25929b993daef88`, which REPRODUCES the value the
assumption library already recorded at
`ancillary_services.dfs_registered_unit_capacity_mw.zone_names.geojson_sha256`. Postcode-district
centroids from postcodes.io (<https://api.postcodes.io>), the open API over the ONS Postcode
Directory. Reproducible: `scripts/derive_dfs_zonal_basis.py --zones`.

**THE CAMPAIGN SPANS TWO ZONES, and each district was checked against a full postcode inside it.**
GL52 2RT and GL52 7RT (Bishops Cleeve, Ash Plant and MRF) zone 10; OX10 6PJ (Ewelme, both records)
zone 10; OX11 0QX (Harwell campus) zone 10; RG10 9XY (Knowl Hill, both records) zone 10; SL3 0EG
(Colnbrook and Tanhouse, one private wire) zone 12. All nine district centroids agreed with their full
postcode. Two independent corroborations fall out of the 26 reference districts resolved in the same
run: Bristol (BS1) and Southampton (SO14) land in zone 10 with Gloucestershire, Oxfordshire and
Berkshire, and London (SW1A) and Medway (ME1) land in zone 12 with Slough.

**What is still open.** A full delivery year of the 2026/27 zonal service (the redesign is four months
old), each site's FULL postcode (a district centroid is what was used, so a district straddling a zone
boundary would resolve to whichever side its centroid falls), and the current DFS Stacking List.

## The all-streams-enabled study basis — a price AND a half-hourly shape for every barred stream (added 2026-08-18, Olly, D55)

**Why this section exists.** The Phase 1 MILP audit proved the dispatch optimiser is at the true
optimum of its model and is *not* trading backwards — and that the negative IRRs come from somewhere
else entirely. Two causes, both outside the solver: opex per kW at sub-100 kW scale, and the fact that
these sites were being optimised inside **a third of the market**. This register's own Modo rows say
so: **88–95% of GB grid-scale BESS revenue is wholesale + balancing + ancillary + capacity**
(2024: £60,689/MW/yr split 45/27/5/22; 2025: £80,088/MW/yr split 39/24/28/9), and every one of those
four is barred to a sub-MW behind-the-meter asset by a 1 MW registered-unit threshold or by an export
consent. The sites were earning only the bill-avoidance slice. This section sources what the other
three-quarters is actually worth **to this asset class**, at the price and the half-hourly shape a MILP
can dispatch against, and it lands in `assumptions/gb_v1_12.yaml` behind the named study basis
`all_streams_enabled_study_v1` (`src/gridq/schemas/study.py`). **Every row moves revenue UP, so every
row is at `needs_review` / low confidence and none of it is an underwriting number.**

| Constant | Value | Units | Basis / conditions | Source | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GB day-ahead price, FULL YEAR at half-hourly granularity (the wholesale shape) | 17,521 half-hours, 2025-08-13 to 2026-08-12. Simple mean **£86.968/MWh**, volume-weighted **£88.728**, median 88.51, p10 40.75, p90 124.70, min −102.92, max 560.81, **427 negative half-hours**. Monthly means: Jan 97.09, Feb 79.72, Mar 95.54, Apr 82.22, May 104.74, Jun 96.19, Jul 105.79, Aug 92.20, Sep 65.32, Oct 72.59, Nov 75.86, Dec 74.77 | £/MWh | Great Britain, Market Index Data (MID), data provider APXMIDP — the APX/EPEX SPOT UK index reported under BSC Section T. Deliberately the **same 12 months as the campaign's metered load**, so price and load describe one year. A 12 × 48 month-by-settlement-period mean matrix is carried in the library as the compressed shape. | Elexon Insights API (public, unauthenticated), `https://data.elexon.co.uk/bmrs/api/v1/balancing/pricing/market-index`, dataset MID, fetched in 53 seven-day windows 2026-08-18; local CSV sha256 `03aee3de3e66de90b8b9006fb81b3aa86fe31b81c6030a63be3572daf2410ee5` | **SUPERSEDES the single settlement day** at `data/fetched/elexon/mid_2026-07-13.csv`, whose 48-half-hour mean of ~£109.6/MWh is **26% above** the full year and is a summer day. Still a HISTORIC OUTTURN, not a forward curve — no forward curve for the delivery years exists in this repo and none was sourced. MID is a transmission-boundary wholesale index: a BTM exporter does not receive it gross. |
| Wholesale arbitrage available to a BTM battery, measured on that year | Perfect foresight, one cycle/day: **1 h £23.64, 2 h £44.20, 4 h £76.39, 8 h £107.54** per kW/yr GROSS. Net of degradation at £21.3/MWh: 15.87 / 28.65 / 45.29 / 45.69. At 80% capture: 12.69 / **22.92** / 36.24 / 36.55. Implied 2 h spread **6.055 p/kWh** | £/kW/yr; p/kWh | Discharge in the n dearest half-hours of each settlement day, charge in the n cheapest, n = 2 × duration_h, charge grossed up by 1/0.88 RTE, negative-spread days skipped. | Own calculation on the MID year above; `scratchpad/stack_calc.py`, 2026-08-18 | **THE DECISIVE NEGATIVE RESULT OF THIS WHOLE SECTION.** 6.055 p/kWh of wholesale spread against a **MEASURED 20.32 p/kWh** behind-the-meter realised spread at bishops_cleeve_mrf (34.12 p/kWh discharge vs 13.80 charge, Phase 1 audit). **The largest slice of the grid-scale index is the LEAST valuable stream for a BTM site**, because the site's own retail spread is 3.4× the wholesale one. Enabling export does not replace bill avoidance, it competes with it for the same cycles and loses. Note also 4 h → 8 h: gross rises £76→£108 but NET is flat (£45.29 vs £45.69) — a model reading the gross column over-sizes duration. |
| GB system (imbalance) price, FULL YEAR half-hourly (the balancing shape) | 17,520 half-hours, same window. Mean **£87.343/MWh**, median 89.98, p10 30.00, p90 137.50, min −97.92, max 800.00, **870 negative half-hours**. Mean premium over MID only **+£0.38/MWh** but mean **absolute** half-hourly divergence **£18.00/MWh**. Hour-of-day mean: 00 82.2, 03 76.0, 06 95.1, 09 79.8, 12 71.1, 15 90.2, **18 116.5**, 21 90.6 | £/MWh | Great Britain, DISEBSP single imbalance price, the marginal price of balancing energy under BSC Section T. Method B: the two Elexon series above; own calculation | **TWO METHODS AGREEING TO 5.4% IS THE STRONGEST CORROBORATION AVAILABLE** without a paid index or a full BOALF × BOD reconstruction under BSC Section T. **DO NOT APPLY A SKIP HAIRCUT TO METHOD A** — the Modo index is realised revenue and skips are already in it; double-haircutting understates BM by ~40%. | The stacking rules, as published — and the two that are NOT exclusivities | **DFS ⊥ BM UNCONDITIONALLY** (a DFS Unit Meter Point "shall not form part of a BM Unit" except a Supplier Base BMU, Procurement Rules v5.0 ¶4.7.6). **DFS ⊥ response/DNO-flex CONDITIONALLY** — barred only where the DFS Stacking List prohibits it (¶4.7.9). **DFS + CM EXPRESSLY PERMITTED** (¶4.7.8(a)). **DC ⊥ DM ⊥ DR** — one dynamic response service per Response Unit; stacking one with the BM IS permitted. **CM stacks with everything:** verbatim, "CM payments can also be stacked with all other relevant Balancing Services". **DNO flex has NO exclusivity:** verbatim, "There are no exclusivity clauses in the contracts so there are no primacy concerns in delivering the service" | n/a (rules) | Great Britain. Cornwall Insight's four-way taxonomy applies: explicitly stackable / implicitly stackable / implicitly unstackable / explicitly unstackable. | NESO, 'Demand Flexibility Service Procurement Rules' v5.0, 18 May 2026, ¶¶4.7.6, 4.7.8(a), 4.7.9, `https://www.neso.energy/document/379391/download`; NESO 'Response Services Procurement Rules' v6 and 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance' v14; Cornwall Insight for NGED, 'Revenue Stacking for Flexibility', Dec 2023, §§9.1 and 13.1 | **TWO PHYSICAL RULES BIND EVEN WHERE THE REGULATIONS PERMIT STACKING, and they are what stop "enable everything" becoming "add everything up": (1) one kW may be committed to at most ONE stream per half-hour; (2) all streams share ONE annual discharge budget** (730 kWh/kW/yr at 2 h). At 2 h, wholesale arbitrage alone consumes 100% of that budget. **DATA ASK: the current DFS Stacking List**, the governing document for ¶4.7.9's conditional limbs, not held in this repo — until it is, DFS and the response/DNO-flex family are modelled non-additively, a conservative choice and not a claim that the rules forbid the stack. |
| Why DFS loses on merit once the other streams exist | DFS central **£7.558/kW/yr** (NESO S2 average) against BM £17.47 + DCL £20.75–20.98 available on the same meter point. **DFS is 5–8× smaller than what choosing it gives up.** | £/kW/yr | Great Britain, NESO's own published per-1-MW winter 2024/25 scenarios (already in this register). | NESO, 'Demand Flexibility Service (DFS)' winter 2024/25 review, 3 July 2025, `https://www.neso.energy/document/363911/download`, pp. 17–18 | **The correct model outcome is that the optimiser DECLINES DFS** — which is exactly why the option must still be given: DFS is the only direct NESO route below 1 MW, so it is what the site earns **if aggregation fails.** A model that hard-codes DFS out cannot answer the aggregator-failure case. **SUPERSEDED AS A MAGNITUDE, 2026-08-19: this row's £7.558 is the winter 2024/25 pre-zonal central case. On the 2026/27 zonal outturn (section above) the campaign's own zone 10 pays £30.34/kW/yr at full delivery and the twelve zones span £8.60–£32.50, median £24.08 — the same order as BM £17.47 and DCL £20.75, not 5–8× below them. The call count rose an order of magnitude while the price fell. So the merit ordering this row asserts is no longer a foregone conclusion, and it is the OPTIMISER's to decide per half-hour, which is what D57 requires and what the dispatch now does.** |
| Parasitic draw: the audit's "2× disagreement" is a SCOPE difference, and both values are now primary-sourced | **Auxiliary load alone 10 kW on a 2,000 kW rated system = 5.0 kW/MW.** **Auxiliary + PCS switching = 20 kW = 1.0% of rated power.** Rest-period DC draw reached **100 kW (5% of rated)** at the tail; auxiliary ran "~1.5% of charge and discharge rated power" (Cycle 1) and "1 to 2.5%" (Cycle 2) | kW/MW; fraction of rated power | Great Britain-relevant but US-measured: a real **2 MW / 4.4 MWh LiFePO4** BESS, tested over multiple cycles. Verbatim: "the DC battery powers auxiliary load and PCS during rest, with average power for each being 10 kW"; "the BESS rated power is 2,000 kW". | PNNL-28379, Crawford, A., Viswanathan, V., Vartanian, C., Mongird, K., Alam, J., Wu, D., Balducci, P., 'Puget Sound Energy Glacier Energy Storage System: An Assessment of Battery Technical Performance', PNNL for US DOE under DE-AC05-76RL01830, July 2019, `https://www.sandia.gov/app/uploads/sites/163/2021/09/PNNL-PSE-Test-Report-Formatted_Final-PDF.pdf` | **RESOLVES THE PHASE 1 AUDIT DEFECT WITHOUT MOVING EITHER NUMBER.** `parasitic_load.standby_kw_per_mw` = 5.0 is the auxiliary-only scope; `battery_opex.parasitic_load_fraction_of_power` = 0.01 is the auxiliary-plus-PCS scope. They never contradicted — they measured different things and neither said which. Both keys move `placeholder` → `needs_review` in gb_v1_12 with this citation. **DIRECTION OF EFFECT ON EVERY REPORTED NUMBER: NONE.** Remaining ask, now narrower: the specific unit's OEM datasheet auxiliary draw at site operating conditions — PNNL shows it is strongly temperature-dependent, with a heating slope 2.5–4× the cooling slope. A single-site US test, not a GB fleet survey. |

### NOT FOUND — searches run 2026-08-18 and recorded so they are not repeated

- **A forward GB wholesale price curve for the delivery years.** Only historic outturn was obtainable
  from public sources. Anything priced off the MID year above is priced off last year's prices.
- **A per-asset GB Balancing Mechanism revenue figure for a sub-MW BTM battery**, and **BM
  offer-acceptance prices at BM Unit level.** The first needs a paid Modo index or a full BOALF × BOD
  settlement reconstruction under BSC Section T; the second needs BMU-level BOD joined to BOALF.
  The DISEBSP system price is used as the marginal-price proxy and is labelled a proxy throughout.
- **An aggregator revenue share, contract term or exclusivity clause published by any GB aggregator.**
  Three searches (2026-08-12, and twice 2026-08-18). Qualitative statements only.
- **A realised distribution of the DC/DM/DR performance factor K for GB batteries.** Unchanged from
  the 2026-08-12 search: NESO publishes the K methodology, the auction results and a monitoring
  template, but no realised distribution. Providers hold their own 20 Hz data; the public record
  does not.
- **~~A current cleared DFS utilisation-price distribution by zone~~ - CLOSED 2026-08-19**, see the
  2026/27 zonal outturn section above: measured per zone from NESO's own live utilisation report. The
  **current DFS Stacking List** remains outstanding from 2026-08-12.
- **An OEM warranty operating envelope (SoC window) for the specific unit.** Searched again; the
  library's 0.15–0.95 remains a published case-study assumption, not a warranty term. **It is left
  UNCHANGED at 0.15/0.95: widening it moves revenue UP by a measured 23.1% and therefore requires a
  document, not a judgement.**
